**14.52.15**  Vāsudeva said: "An effort was made by me, O brāhmaṇa, toward brotherhood among the Kauravas; but those who delighted in unrighteousness could not be reconciled by me."

**14.52.16**  "And so they all met their destruction, together with their sons and kinsmen. What is ordained cannot be overstepped by wit or by strength, O great sage; surely all this is known to you, O sinless one."

**14.52.17**  "They transgressed my counsel, and that of Bhīṣma and of Vidura; and so, falling upon one another, they went to the abode of Yama."

**14.52.18**  "Only the five Pāṇḍavas survive, their friends slain, their sons slain; and the sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra are all destroyed, together with their sons and kinsmen."

**14.52.19**  When Kṛṣṇa had spoken these words, Uttaṅka, filled with intense wrath, his eyes starting open in fury, answered him in turn.

**14.52.20**  "Since, though you were able, O Kṛṣṇa, you did not save the Kurus and Pāṇḍavas, your dear kinsmen, therefore I shall surely curse you, without doubt."

**14.52.21**  "And since you did not by force restrain and turn them back, therefore, overcome with indignation, I shall curse you, O slayer of Madhu."

**14.52.22**  "For though you were able, O Mādhava, you dealt deceitfully and so betrayed those foremost of the Kurus, in that you stood by and ignored them."

**14.52.23**  Vāsudeva said: "Hear from me in full what I shall tell you, O delight of the Bhṛgus, and accept my conciliation as well, for you are an ascetic, O scion of Bhṛgu."

**14.52.24**  "When you have heard this teaching of the supreme Self, you will this very day release your curse; and no man is able to overcome me with any slight austerity."

**14.52.25**  "Nor do I wish the destruction of your austerity, O best of those who recite their prayers; your austerity is vast and blazing, and your teachers too have been gratified."

**14.52.26**  "I know your chastity, kept from boyhood, O best of the twice-born; and therefore I do not wish the waste of austerity won by you through such hardship."

**14.53.1**  Uttaṅka said: "Tell me truly, O Keśava, the blameless teaching of the supreme Self; having heard it, I shall pronounce what is better — or else my curse upon you, O Janārdana."

**14.53.2**  Vāsudeva said: "Know tamas, rajas, and sattva to be conditions resting upon me; and know the Rudras and the Vasus too to spring from me, O twice-born."

**14.53.3**  "In me abide all beings, and in all beings I abide — thus understand it; let there be no doubt in you on this matter."

**14.53.4**  "Likewise know all the hosts of Daityas, the Yakṣas, Rākṣasas, and serpents, and the Gandharvas and Apsarases too, to spring from me, O twice-born."

**14.53.5**  "What they call the existent and the non-existent, the unmanifest and the manifest, the imperishable and the perishable — all this consists of me."

**14.53.6**  "The duties ordained for the four orders of life, O sage, and the rites pertaining to the gods — know all this to consist of me."

**14.53.7**  "The non-existent, and that which is both existent and non-existent — this universe — is beyond both the existent and the non-existent; and beyond that there is nothing higher than the eternal God of gods."

**14.53.8**  "Know me to be the Vedas born from the syllable Oṃ, O foremost of the Bhṛgus, and the sacrificial post, the Soma, and the gratification of the thirty gods at the sacrifice."

**14.53.9**  "Know me too as the hotṛ priest and the oblation, O delight of the Bhṛgus; the adhvaryu, the officiant, and the most perfectly consecrated offering."

**14.53.10**  "The udgātṛ too praises me with the sound of his chants at the great sacrifice; and in the rites of expiation, O brāhmaṇa, the foremost of the twice-born, uttering words of peace and blessing, ever praise me as the maker of all."

**14.53.11**  "Know Dharma to be my son, my firstborn, O best of the twice-born — born of my mind, beloved, O brāhmaṇa, whose very nature is compassion toward all beings."

**14.53.12**  "There I dwell among men who are active and among those who have renounced, passing through many wombs, O best of the twice-born."

**14.53.13**  "For the sake of guarding righteousness and for establishing righteousness, I do so in those various guises and forms throughout the three worlds, O scion of Bhṛgu."

**14.53.14**  "I am Viṣṇu, I am Brahmā, and I am Śakra, the origin and the dissolution; I am the creator of the whole multitude of beings, and their destroyer as well."

**14.53.15**  "When all are caught up in unrighteousness, I bind together the embankment of righteousness; and as each age decays, I enter those various wombs, desiring the welfare of the creatures."

**14.53.16**  "When I dwell in the womb of the gods, O delight of the Bhṛgus, then I act in all things like a god, without doubt."

**14.53.17**  "And when I dwell in the womb of the Gandharvas, O delight of the Bhṛgus, then I perform all my acts like a Gandharva, O scion of Bhṛgu."

**14.53.18**  "And when I am in the womb of the Nāgas, then I dwell as a Nāga; and in the wombs of the Yakṣas and Rākṣasas I move about accordingly."

**14.53.19**  "But when I dwell in human form and beseech them piteously, those men, sunk in delusion, do not heed my salutary word."

**14.53.20**  "Pointing to a great peril, I terrified the Kurus; and becoming, as it were, enraged, I once again showed them things as they truly were."

**14.53.21**  "They, joined here with unrighteousness and overtaken by the law of Time, were slain in battle through righteousness and have gone to heaven, without doubt."

**14.53.22**  "And the Pāṇḍavas too have won renown throughout the worlds, O best of the twice-born. All this has been declared to you, even that about which you have questioned me."

**14.54.1**  Uttaṅka said: "I recognize you, O Janārdana, as the maker of the world; surely this is your grace — of that I have no doubt."

**14.54.2**  "My mind is wholly serene, fixed upon your being, O Acyuta; and my anger has departed — know this, O scorcher of foes."

**14.54.3**  "But if I am worthy of any favor from you, O Janārdana, I wish to behold your sovereign form; show me that."

**14.54.4**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Then the wise one, glad at heart, displayed to him that eternal Vaiṣṇava form, which Dhanaṃjaya had beheld.

**14.54.5**  He beheld the great-souled one, the mighty-armed, in his universal form; and the brāhmaṇa was filled with wonder upon seeing that sovereign shape.

**14.54.6**  Uttaṅka said: "O Maker of all, homage be to you, whose form is such as this — by your feet the earth is pervaded, and by your head the sky is covered."

**14.54.7**  "The space that lies between heaven and earth is filled by your belly, and the quarters are encompassed by your arms; you are all this, O Acyuta."

**14.54.8**  "Withdraw again, O god, this imperishable, supreme form; I wish to behold you once more, the eternal one, in your own shape."

**14.54.9**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Govinda, serene of soul, said to him, O Janamejaya, "Choose a boon"; and then Uttaṅka spoke this to him.

**14.54.10**  "This very thing is boon enough today from you, O greatly resplendent one: that I behold this form of yours, O Kṛṣṇa, the origin and the dissolution."

**14.54.11**  Kṛṣṇa said to him again: "Do not hesitate in this matter; this must surely be done, for the sight of me is never in vain."

**14.54.12**  Uttaṅka said: "If you deem this must surely be done, O lord, then I wish for water wherever I desire it; for in these deserts that is hard to come by."

**14.54.13**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Then, withdrawing that splendor, the Lord said to Uttaṅka, "When you have need, you are to think of me"; and having so spoken, he departed for Dvārakā.

**14.54.14**  Then once, the venerable Uttaṅka, longing for water and parched with thirst, wandered about in the desert and called Acyuta to mind.

**14.54.15**  Then the wise one saw, in that desert, a naked outcaste hunter, smeared with filth and mire, surrounded by a pack of dogs.

**14.54.16**  He was terrible, his sword girt on, bearing arrows and a bow; and beneath him, from his lower stream, that best of the twice-born saw abundant water.

**14.54.17**  And the hunter, as if reading his very thought, said with a laugh: "Come, Uttaṅka, accept water from me, O foremost of the Bhṛgus; for great indeed is my pity at the sight of you stricken with thirst."

**14.54.18**  Thus addressed by him, the sage did not welcome that water; and the wise one assailed the unfailing one with harsh words.

**14.54.19**  Again and again the hunter said to him, "Drink"; yet he did not drink, being full of anger, his inner self troubled.

**14.54.20**  Thus firmly refused by that great-souled one, he, together with the dogs, O great king, vanished then and there.

**14.54.21**  Uttaṅka, having seen this, was then abashed in mind, and thought himself deceived by Kṛṣṇa, the slayer of foes.

**14.54.22**  Then by that very road came the mighty-armed bearer of the conch, discus, and mace; and Uttaṅka spoke to him.

**14.54.23**  "It was not fitting, O best of men, that you should offer such water to the foremost of brāhmaṇas, from the stream of an outcaste hunter, O lord."

**14.54.24**  When these words were spoken, the wise Janārdana of great understanding, consoling Uttaṅka with gentle speech, said this.

**14.54.25**  "In just such a form as was fitting and chosen for the giving, indeed I gave it; but you did not understand it."

**14.54.26**  "For your sake I told the wielder of the thunderbolt, Puraṃdara the lord: 'Give to Uttaṅka the nectar of immortality in the form of water.'"

**14.54.27**  "The king of the gods said to me: 'No mortal may attain immortality; grant him some other boon' — and this more than once, O delight of the Bhṛgus."

**14.54.28**  "But by me the lord of Śacī was told, 'The nectar is to be given'; and the king of the gods, having conciliated me, spoke again as follows."

**14.54.29**  "'If it must indeed be given, O greatly resplendent one, then having become an outcaste hunter, I shall bestow the nectar upon the great-souled scion of Bhṛgu.'"

**14.54.30**  "'If on these terms the scion of Bhṛgu accepts the nectar this very day, then I go to bestow the nectar upon him, O lord; but if I am refused by him, I shall not give it' — so he said, O scion of Bhṛgu."

**14.54.31**  "Having thus made the compact, Vāsava presented himself in that form, and even as he was offering the nectar he was refused by you. In the guise of a Caṇḍāla was the lord; great indeed is your transgression."

**14.54.32**  "But what it is yet in my power to do for your wish, O unassailable one — your longing for water I shall make fruitful."

**14.54.33**  "On whatever days you shall feel a longing for water, O brāhmaṇa, on those days the rain-clouds in the desert shall become laden with water.

**14.54.34**  And they shall yield you sweet, savory water, O delight of the Bhṛgus; named 'the clouds of Uttaṅka,' they shall come to be renowned."

**14.54.35**  Thus addressed by Kṛṣṇa, the brāhmaṇa was filled with delight; and even today, O Bhārata, the clouds of Uttaṅka rain down upon the desert.

**14.55.1**  Janamejaya said: With what austerity was Uttaṅka endowed, that great ascetic of mighty penance, who was minded to lay a curse upon Viṣṇu, the all-powerful?

**14.55.2**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Uttaṅka was endowed with great austerity, O Janamejaya; devoted to his teacher, that radiant one revered no other being at all.

**14.55.3**  And this was the heart's desire of all the sages' sons, O Bhārata: 'May I attain such service to the teacher as is Uttaṅka's.'

**14.55.4**  But among the many disciples of Gautama, O Janamejaya, it was for Uttaṅka that the greatest affection and tenderness then arose.

**14.55.5**  By his self-restraint and purity, by his vigorous toil, and by his perfect attendance, Gautama was made well pleased with him.

**14.55.6**  Now Gautama, having given leave to his thousands of disciples, out of his supreme affection did not wish to grant leave to Uttaṅka.

**14.55.7**  Old age, my son, by degrees came upon that great sage, yet the sage, fond of his teacher, did not at that time perceive it.

**14.55.8**  Then once, O lord of kings, Uttaṅka went forth to fetch firewood, and he brought back a great load of wood.

**14.55.9**  But overwhelmed by the burden, O subduer of foes, weary and famished, he flung the load of wood down upon the ground, O king.

**14.55.10**  A matted lock of his hair, bright as silver, had caught upon the wood, and so it fell along with the firewood onto the surface of the earth.

**14.55.11**  Then the descendant of Bhṛgu, crushed by the burden and seized by hunger, beholding that condition of his years, wept aloud in anguish.

**14.55.12**  Then the teacher's daughter, whose eyes were like lotus-petals, fair-hipped and large-eyed, knowing the law, at her father's command and with bowed head, caught his tears in her hand.

**14.55.13**  Her two hands were scorched by those drops of tears as they fell; for not even the earth was able to bear those falling tears.

**14.55.14**  Then Gautama, his heart kindly disposed, said to the brāhmaṇa Uttaṅka: "Why, my son, is your mind today so overcome with grief? Speak freely, O brāhmaṇa-sage; I wish to hear your words."

**14.55.15**  Uttaṅka said: With my mind fixed upon you, with the wish to do what pleases you, devoted here to you and following your every disposition—

**14.55.16**  this old age of mine I never perceived, nor did I ever know pleasure; for a hundred years I have dwelt here, yet you did not grant me leave to depart.

**14.55.17**  For by you, O best of the twice-born, disciples lesser than I, by the hundreds and by the thousands, were granted leave and set forth duly equipped.

**14.55.18**  Gautama said: Bound by my affection for you, and by your service to your teacher, a great span of time slipped by unperceived by me, O bull among the twice-born.

**14.55.19**  But if today you have a mind to depart, O descendant of Bhṛgu, then take your leave from me and go home; do not delay.

**14.55.20**  Uttaṅka said: What fee for the teacher shall I render? Tell me, O best of the twice-born; having presented it, I would go, granted leave by you, O mighty one.

**14.55.21**  Gautama said: The satisfaction of teachers is itself the fee, so the good declare; and at your conduct, O brāhmaṇa, I am content, there is no doubt of it.

**14.55.22**  And know me to be so well content, O scion of Bhṛgu, that this very day you shall become young, a youth of sixteen years.

**14.55.23**  I give you as wife my own maiden daughter, O twice-born; for none other but she is fit to attend upon your splendor.

**14.55.24**  Then, having become young, he accepted that illustrious maiden; and, granted leave by his teacher, he spoke to his teacher's wife:

**14.55.25**  "What shall I render to you? Appoint me to a teacher's-fee task; for I long to do what pleases you, even at the cost of my life and my wealth."

**14.55.26**  "Whatever jewel in this world is hard to win and most wondrous, that would I bring by my austerity; of this I have no doubt."

**14.55.27**  Ahalyā said: I am ever well content with you, my son, together with the venerable one; let that suffice. Blessings on you; go, my child, as you wish.

**14.55.28**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: But Uttaṅka, O great king, spoke yet again these words: "Command me, O mother; for there is a service of mine that should please you."

**14.55.29**  Ahalyā said: The celestial jeweled earrings, well known to be the wife of Saudāsa's—bring those to me; blessings on you, and so the teacher's-fee will be well performed.

**14.55.30**  Promising "So be it," he set forth, O Janamejaya, to fetch those earrings then, for the pleasure of his teacher's wife.

**14.55.31**  Then Uttaṅka, that bull among brāhmaṇas, went swiftly forth to beg the jeweled earrings of Saudāsa the man-eater.

**14.55.32**  But Gautama said to his wife: "Uttaṅka is not to be seen today." Thus questioned, she told him that he had gone for the sake of the earrings.

**14.55.33**  Then he said to his wife: "This was not rightly done by you; that king, being under a curse, will surely slay the brāhmaṇa."

**14.55.34**  Ahalyā said: It was unknowingly that I appointed the brāhmaṇa to this, O venerable one; yet through your grace no harm at all shall come to him.

**14.55.35**  Thus addressed, Gautama said to his wife, "So be it." And Uttaṅka, meanwhile, beheld that king in a lonely forest.

**14.56.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Beholding that king in such a state, dreadful to look upon, wearing a long beard and drenched with the blood of men,

**14.56.2**  the brāhmaṇa felt no fear; but the king, mighty in splendor, a striker of terror, like Yama himself, rose up and addressed him:

**14.56.3**  "By good fortune, auspicious one, you have come into my presence at the sixth hour, while I was seeking food, O best of the twice-born—as my food."

**14.56.4**  Uttaṅka said: O king, know me to be one come hither while engaged on a teacher's errand; and the wise declare that one bound on a teacher's errand must not be harmed.

**14.56.5**  The king said: At the sixth hour my meal is ordained, O best of the twice-born, and it cannot be relinquished by me today, hungry as I am.

**14.56.6**  Uttaṅka said: So be it, O great king; but let a compact be made with me. Having accomplished the teacher's errand, I shall return into your power.

**14.56.7**  And the object I have promised to my teacher, O best of kings, depends upon you, O lord of kings; for that I beg of you, O ruler of men.

**14.56.8**  For you bestow jewels of every kind upon the foremost of brāhmaṇas; you are a giver, O tiger among men, a worthy vessel of giving here on earth—and know me too to be a worthy vessel for receiving, O best of kings.

**14.56.9**  Having presented to my teacher the object that depends on you, O subduer of foes, by this compact, O lord of kings, I shall return into your power.

**14.56.10**  Truly do I promise you this; herein there is no falsehood at all. Never before have I spoken untruth even in jest—how then otherwise?

**14.56.11**  Saudāsa said: If the teacher's errand that depends on you rests with me, then it is as good as done. And if I am one fit to be received from, tell me now what it is.

**14.56.12**  Uttaṅka said: You are ever deemed by me fit to receive from, O bull among men; and so I have come to you to beg the jeweled earrings.

**14.56.13**  Saudāsa said: The lovely jeweled earrings, O brāhmaṇa-sage, are my wife's; choose some other object, and that I shall give you, O man of good vows.

**14.56.14**  Uttaṅka said: Enough of your evasions! If we are of any worth in your eyes, give me the earrings, and be a man of your word, O king.

**14.56.15**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Thus addressed, the king spoke again to Uttaṅka these words: "Go, O excellent one, and at my word say to the queen, 'Give them.'

**14.56.16**  She, addressed by you with my words, that lady of bright smile, will surely give you the earrings, O best of the twice-born; of this there is no doubt."

**14.56.17**  Uttaṅka said: Where can your wife be seen by me, O ruler of men? Or why do you not yourself go to your wife?

**14.56.18**  Saudāsa said: You shall see her today at some forest-stream; for at the sixth hour she cannot be looked upon by me today.

**14.56.19**  Uttaṅka, thus addressed, went forth, O bull of the Bharatas; and beholding Madayantī, he made known his errand.

**14.56.20**  Hearing the message of Saudāsa, that large-eyed lady then made reply to the deeply wise Uttaṅka, O Janamejaya:

**14.56.21**  "So it is, O great brāhmaṇa; you speak no untruth, O sinless one. But you must bring some token of recognition hither.

**14.56.22**  For these celestial jeweled earrings of mine—gods and yakṣas and great serpents alike, eager to carry them off by every kind of stratagem, ever scheme to seize their unguarded moments.

**14.56.23**  Should this jewel be set down upon the ground, the serpents would come and bear it away; should it be worn while one is unclean, the yakṣas would; and should you fall under the sway of sleep, the gods would assail you.

**14.56.24**  Against all these openings, otherwise unassailable, O bull among the twice-born, it must be borne with vigilance, safe from gods, rākṣasas, and serpents.

**14.56.25**  By day they ooze gold, O best of the twice-born, and by night as well; at night they shine, outshining the radiance of the constellations and stars.

**14.56.26**  For one who wears these, O reverend one, whence comes any fear of hunger or thirst? Nor is there ever any fear from poison, fire, or wild beasts.

**14.56.27**  When worn by one who is small, these too become small to fit, and when worn by one of proportionate size, they grow to that measure.

**14.56.28**  Of such a kind are these earrings of mine, most highly prized, renowned throughout the three worlds. Therefore bring the token of recognition."

**14.57.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Approaching Mitrasaha, he asked for the token of recognition; and that best of the Ikṣvākus thereupon gave him the token.

**14.57.2**  Saudāsa said: This course is not a safe one, yet no other course exists. Knowing this to be my judgment, give him the jeweled earrings.

**14.57.3**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Thus addressed, Uttaṅka repeated to her the husband's words; and hearing them, she then gave him those jeweled earrings.

**14.57.4**  Having obtained the earrings, he spoke again to the king: "What was that secret message? I wish to hear it, O king."

**14.57.5**  Saudāsa said: By their very nature the kṣatriyas among men honor the brāhmaṇas; yet from the brāhmaṇas many misfortunes also arise for us.

**14.57.6**  Bowed down before the twice-born though I was, I came to ruin through a brāhmaṇa; and I see no other path, with Madayantī as my helpmate, for reaching heaven's gate or for abiding here, O best of the twice-born.

**14.57.7**  For a king, above all, who is at odds with the twice-born is unable to abide in the world of men, or to prosper in happiness after death.

**14.57.8**  Therefore at your wish these very earrings of ours have been given by me; now make fruitful for me the compact that you made with me today.

**14.57.9**  Uttaṅka said: O king, so shall I do; I shall return into your power. But I am resolved to put a certain question to you, O scorcher of foes.

**14.57.10**  Saudāsa said: Speak, O brāhmaṇa, as you please; I shall give you an answer. I shall today resolve your doubt; herein I have no hesitation.

**14.57.11**  Uttaṅka said: Those who discern the niceties of the law declare a friend to be one true in word; and whoever is false toward his friends, him they know to be a thief.

**14.57.12**  You, sir, have today come into friendship with me, O king; therefore grant me even-tempered counsel, O best of the wise.

**14.57.13**  I have today attained my object, and you are a devourer of men. Tell me whether it is fitting or not for me to return into your presence.

**14.57.14**  Saudāsa said: If it is fitting that I should speak here, O most excellent of the twice-born, then, O best of brāhmaṇas, you should by no means come into my presence at all.

**14.57.15**  Thus, O scion of the line of Bhṛgu, I see what is best for you; for were you to come to me, brahmin, your death would be certain.

**14.57.16**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Thus addressed by the wise king with words fit and salutary, he took leave of the monarch and set out toward Ahalyā.

**14.57.17**  Having taken the divine earrings, that doer of kindnesses for his teacher's wife sped with great swiftness toward the hermitage of Gautama.

**14.57.18**  As Madayantī had instructed concerning their safekeeping, so he bound the two earrings and carried them wrapped in a black antelope skin.

**14.57.19**  At one point, overtaken by hunger, he saw a bilva tree laden with a burden of fruit, and, driven by hunger, he climbed it.

**14.57.20**  He hung upon its branches that very black antelope skin, O subduer of foes, in which the two earrings had been bound by the best of the twice-born.

**14.57.21**  When its binding came loose and the black antelope skin fell to the ground, a certain serpent there caught sight of those jeweled earrings.

**14.57.22**  Sprung from the lineage of Airāvata, that swift serpent then seized the earrings in its mouth and entered into an anthill.

**14.57.23**  Seeing the earrings being carried off by the serpent, distraught and grieving, in extreme wrath he fell down from the tree.

**14.57.24**  Then that bull among the twice-born, his limbs inflamed with anger and indignation, took up a wooden staff and began to dig open the anthill.

**14.57.25**  The Earth, unable to bear his unbearable force, her body battered by the wooden staff, trembled violently and was sorely afflicted.

**14.57.26**  And so the seer among brahmins kept digging at the floor of the earth, resolved in his determination to carve a path to the world of the serpents.

**14.57.27**  Then the wielder of the thunderbolt, of mighty splendor, came to that place in his chariot yoked with bays and beheld the best of the twice-born.

**14.57.28**  Taking on the form of a brahmin, and grieved by his grief, he said to Uttaṅka: 'My son, this cannot be accomplished by you.'

**14.57.29**  'For the world of the serpents lies thousands of leagues from here; I do not think this task of yours can be achieved with a wooden staff.'

**14.57.30**  Uttaṅka said: O brahmin, if the earrings cannot be recovered by me from the world of the serpents, I shall give up my life before your very eyes, O best of the twice-born.

**14.57.31**  When the wielder of the thunderbolt could not turn his resolve otherwise, he then charged the staff with the power of his thunderbolt weapon.

**14.57.32**  Then the Earth, rent open by those thunderbolt strokes, made a passage to the world of the serpents, O Janamejaya.

**14.57.33**  By that road he then entered the world of the serpents, and he beheld that world stretching thousands of leagues.

**14.57.34**  It was furnished, O fortunate one, with heaps of divine ramparts adorned with gems and pearls and fashioned of pure gold.

**14.57.35**  He saw pools with crystal stairways, rivers of limpid water, and many trees thronged with flocks of various birds.

**14.57.36**  And that perpetuator of the line of Bhṛgu beheld the gateway of that world, five leagues broad and a hundred leagues in length.

**14.57.37**  But Uttaṅka, gazing upon the world of the serpents, grew downcast, and once more, my son, he despaired of recovering the earrings.

**14.57.38**  There a horse, black with a white tail, copper of mouth and eye, blazing as it were with radiance, spoke to him, O Kaurava.

**14.57.39**  'Blow into this vent of mine, brahmin, and then you shall obtain them; for here the son of Airāvata has carried off your earrings.'

**14.57.40**  'Feel no revulsion in this matter, my son, by any means; for this very thing you yourself performed once in the hermitage of Gautama.'

**14.57.41**  Uttaṅka said: How should I know you, in connection with my preceptor's hermitage? And what I did there before, that I wish to hear.

**14.57.42**  The horse said: Know me to be the teacher of your teacher, the blazing Fire, the knower of all that is born; for I, my child, was ever honored by you for your teacher's sake.

**14.57.43**  Always was I worshipped, brahmin, by you in purity, O joy of the Bhṛgus; therefore I shall do what is best for you. Do as I say, and tarry not.

**14.57.44**  Thus addressed by the Fire of varied splendor, Uttaṅka did accordingly; and the Fire, whose flame is fed with ghee, well pleased, blazed up with the will to consume.

**14.57.45**  Then from the pores of his body, as he was being blown upon, O Bhārata, there arose a dense smoke that struck terror into the world of the serpents.

**14.57.46**  By that smoke, swelling all at once, O Bhārata, in the world of the serpents, great king, nothing whatsoever could be made out.

**14.57.47**  All the dwelling of Airāvata was filled with cries of dismay, as were the abodes of the serpents led by Vāsuki, O Janamejaya.

**14.57.48**  Choked with smoke, the mansions shone no more, O Bhārata, like forests and mountains shrouded in mist.

**14.57.49**  Their eyes reddened by the smoke, scorched by the heat of the fire, they came to learn the purpose of the Bhārgava of surpassing splendor.

**14.57.50**  And having heard the resolve of that great seer of fierce splendor, all of them, their minds thrown into confusion, paid him honor according to the rite.

**14.57.51**  All the serpents, with joined palms, the aged and the young foremost among them, bowed their heads and said: 'Be gracious, O blessed one.'

**14.57.52**  Having appeased the brahmin and offered him water for his feet and the honor-offering, those serpents presented to that most worshipful one the two divine earrings.

**14.57.53**  Then, honored by the serpents, the mighty Uttaṅka there circled the Fire to the right and set out for that house of his teacher.

**14.57.54**  Going in haste, O king, to the dwelling of Gautama, he then presented the two divine earrings to his teacher's wife, O sinless one.

**14.57.55**  Thus by that great soul, O Janamejaya, after he had traversed the three worlds, were those divine jeweled earrings brought back.

**14.57.56**  Of such power was that sage Uttaṅka, O bull of the Bharatas, endowed with supreme austerity, about whom you question me.

**14.58.1**  Janamejaya said: After granting the boon to Uttaṅka, O best of the twice-born, what did the mighty-armed Govinda of great renown do thereafter?

**14.58.2**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Having granted the boon to Uttaṅka, Govinda set out together with Sātyaki for Dvārakā itself, drawn by great, swift-paced horses.

**14.58.3**  Crossing lakes and rivers and woodlands of many kinds, he then reached the lovely city of Dvāravatī.

**14.58.4**  While the festival of Mount Raivataka was in progress, O great king, the lotus-eyed one approached, followed by Yuyudhāna.

**14.58.5**  That mountain, adorned and made wondrous with diverse forms, shone on every side with golden lattices, O bull among men.

**14.58.6**  With choice golden garlands, with flowers, and with cloths, and with wish-granting trees on every side, was that great mountain bedecked.

**14.58.7**  Resplendent throughout with golden lamp-trees, in the regions of its caves and cascades it became as bright as day.

**14.58.8**  With gay banners hung with bells on every side, resounding with the voices of men and women, it seemed to break into song; exceedingly fair to behold it was, like Meru thronged with hosts of sages.

**14.58.9**  The sound of women and men, flushed with drink and joyous in form, singing upon that lord of mountains, seemed, O Bhārata, to touch the very heavens.

**14.58.10**  Thronged with the shouts and roars of the heedless, the tipsy, and the wholly drunken, and with cries of merriment, the place became most enchanting.

**14.58.11**  Lovely with its markets and stalls, rich in food to chew and food to eat and in pleasure-grounds, heaped with garments and garlands, and resounding with lute and flute and drum, it was.

**14.58.12**  With wine and mead and with food to chew and to eat, given out without ceasing to the poor, the blind, the wretched, and the like, the most auspicious festival of that great mountain shone forth.

**14.58.13**  Furnished with holy retreats, O hero, frequented by doers of holy deeds, the pleasure-ground of the Vṛṣṇi warriors at the festival of Raivataka — that mountain, crowded with dwellings, shone like the world of the gods.

**14.58.14**  And then, gaining the presence of Kṛṣṇa, O bull of the Bharatas, that king of mountains became the very likeness of the abode of Śakra.

**14.58.15**  Then Govinda, being honored, entered the splendid palace, while Sātyaki too went off to his own dwelling.

**14.58.16**  Glad of heart, after a long sojourn abroad, he entered, having wrought a deed hard to accomplish, like Vāsava among the Dānavas.

**14.58.17**  When the scion of the Vṛṣṇis had arrived, the Bhojas, Vṛṣṇis, and Andhakas then came forth to that great soul, as the gods come to the lord of a hundred sacrifices.

**14.58.18**  That wise one, having honored them and inquired after their welfare, then, well pleased, made his obeisance to his father and his mother.

**14.58.19**  Embraced by the two of them and comforted, the mighty-armed one sat down, surrounded by all those Vṛṣṇis.

**14.58.20**  Then, rested, his feet bathed, that Kṛṣṇa of great splendor, when questioned by his father, recounted the great battle.

**14.59.1**  Vasudeva said: I have heard, O scion of the Vṛṣṇis, of that most marvelous battle, my son, continually, in the openings of men's tales as they spoke.

**14.59.2**  But you are one who saw it with your own eyes and who knows what was done, O mighty-armed one; therefore tell me the battle truly as it was, O sinless one.

**14.59.3**  How that unequaled war of the great-souled Pāṇḍavas was waged with Bhīṣma, Karṇa, Kṛpa, Droṇa, Śalya, and the rest —

**14.59.4**  — and with many other warriors skilled in arms, of varied garb and form, dwellers in many lands.

**14.59.5**  Thus addressed by his father, the lotus-eyed one, in his mother's presence, related how the heroes of the Kurus had met their end in the battle.

**14.59.6**  Vāsudeva said: The most wondrous deeds of those great-souled warriors, by reason of their multitude, could not be reckoned even in a hundred years.

**14.59.7**  But hear from me, as I tell it in brief and by its chief points, the deeds of those lords of the earth, faithfully, O you of godlike radiance.

**14.59.8**  Bhīṣma the Kuru was the commander of the Kauravas, lord of eleven armies, as Vāsava is over the gods.

**14.59.9**  Śikhaṇḍin was the leader of the sons of Pāṇḍu, lord of seven armies; the wise one was guarded by the wise Savyasācin.

**14.59.10**  For ten days raged that battle of those great souls, the Kurus and the Pāṇḍavas, vast and hair-raising.

**14.59.11**  Then Śikhaṇḍin, together with the wielder of the Gāṇḍīva, struck down with many arrows the son of Gaṅgā as he refused to fight in that great battle.

**14.59.12**  Then that sage, lying upon his bed of arrows, made his end, forsaking the southern course of the sun and awaiting the coming of the northern.

**14.59.13**  Then Droṇa, the best of those versed in weapons, became commander, the great hero of the Kaurava lord, as Kāvya was of the king of the Daityas.

**14.59.14**  That best of the twice-born, glorying in battle, was surrounded by the nine armies that remained, and guarded by Kṛpa, the Bull, and the others.

**14.59.15**  Dhṛṣṭadyumna, great knower of weapons, became the leader of the Pāṇḍavas, that splendid one guarded by Bhīma as Varuṇa is by his ally.

**14.59.16**  Surrounded by five hosts, that high-minded one, eager to come at Droṇa, remembering the insults done to his father, performed a great feat in the fray.

**14.59.17**  In that encounter between Droṇa and the son of Pṛṣata, those lords of the earth, heroes come from many quarters, for the most part met their death.

**14.59.18**  For five days that battle raged, most terrible; then Droṇa, worn out, fell into the power of Dhṛṣṭadyumna.

**14.59.19**  Then Karṇa became the commander of Duryodhana's army, surrounded in battle by the five akṣauhiṇīs that yet remained.

**14.59.20**  The three divisions of the sons of Pāṇḍu, guarded by Bībhatsu, stood firm in their array, though most of their foremost heroes had been slain.

**14.59.21**  Then, having closed with Pārtha as a moth flies into the flame, the son of the charioteer met his dissolution on the second dreadful day.

**14.59.22**  When Karṇa was slain, the Kauravas, dispirited and stripped of vigor, with their three remaining akṣauhiṇīs surrounded the lord of the Madras.

**14.59.23**  The Pāṇḍavas, most of whose mounts had been killed and who too were dispirited, surrounded Yudhiṣṭhira with the single akṣauhiṇī that remained.

**14.59.24**  Yudhiṣṭhira, king of the Kurus, slew the king of the Madras, accomplishing on that half-day a deed most hard to do.

**14.59.25**  When Śalya was slain, the high-minded Sahadeva, of boundless valor, struck down Śakuni, the very kindler of that strife.

**14.59.26**  When Śakuni was killed, the king, the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, sore at heart, withdrew, mace in hand, most of his soldiers slain.

**14.59.27**  The mighty Bhīmasena, filled with wrath, pursued him, and beheld him standing within the waters of the Dvaipāyana lake.

**14.59.28**  Then, surrounding him on every side with the army that remained, the five Pāṇḍavas, exultant, sat down close about him as he stood in the lake.

**14.59.29**  Plunging swiftly through the water, sorely pierced by their arrows of words, he rose up, mace in hand, and stood ready for battle.

**14.59.30**  Then that king, the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, was slain in the great combat by Bhīmasena's prowess, before the eyes of the rulers of the earth.

**14.59.31**  Thereafter the army of the Pāṇḍavas, asleep at night in its camp, was slaughtered by the son of Droṇa, who could not bear his father's slaying.

**14.59.32**  Their sons slain, their forces slain, their friends slain, the five Pāṇḍavas alone survived, together with me and with Yuyudhāna as a sixth.

**14.59.33**  The son of Droṇa, together with Kṛpa and the Bhoja, escaped from the war; and Yuyutsu the Kaurava too was spared, having taken refuge with the Pāṇḍavas.

**14.59.34**  When Suyodhana, lord of the Kurus, was slain along with all his kin, both Vidura and Saṃjaya came and waited upon the King of Righteousness.

**14.59.35**  Thus, O lord, did that war last eighteen days, wherein those guardians of the earth, being slain, came to dwell in heaven.

**14.59.36**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: As they listened to that hair-raising tale, O great king, the Vṛṣṇis were then beset by anguish, joy, and torment all at once.

**14.60.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: As the mighty Vāsudeva was recounting that great Bhārata war, there before his father, at the close of his telling—

**14.60.2**  —the hero, O Bhārata, the high-minded one, passed over the slaying of Abhimanyu, that it might bring no grief to Vasudeva.

**14.60.3**  For the wise one thought: let not Vasudeva, hearing of the dread slaying of his daughter's son, be consumed by sorrow and grief.

**14.60.4**  But Subhadrā, marking that he had passed over the slaying of her son in battle, cried, "Kṛṣṇa, tell of the death of Saubhadra!" and fell upon the ground.

**14.60.5**  Vasudeva then saw her fallen upon the earth, and at the very sight he too sank to the ground, swooning with grief.

**14.60.6**  Then Vasudeva, O great king, overcome with sorrow and grief at the slaying of his daughter's son, spoke these words to Kṛṣṇa:

**14.60.7**  "Surely, lotus-eyed one, you are renowned upon earth as a speaker of truth, O slayer of foes—why then do you not tell me today of my grandson's death?

**14.60.8**  Tell me truly, O lord, of the death of your sister's son. How was that boy, whose eyes were like your own, slain by the foe in battle?

**14.60.9**  Hard indeed it is, O Vārṣṇeya, for men ever to die before their time has come—since my heart for grief does not shatter into a hundred pieces!

**14.60.10**  What did he say to you in the battle concerning his mother Subhadrā, and concerning me, O lotus-eyed one—that restless-eyed boy so dear to me?

**14.60.11**  He was not slain by the enemy turning his back upon the fight, was he? Tell me, Govinda, that he never made his face turn away in that combat.

**14.60.12**  For that mighty-splendored lordling, O Kṛṣṇa, would boast as it were before me, and in his boyish way would tell of his own victories.

**14.60.13**  That the boy was not maimed and laid low upon the earth by Droṇa, Karṇa, Kṛpa and the rest—this tell me, O Keśava.

**14.60.14**  For my daughter's little son would ever vie in war with Droṇa, with Bhīṣma, and with Karṇa, the foremost of chariot-warriors.

**14.60.15**  As his father lamented thus at length, sore stricken, Govinda, more stricken still, spoke these words:

**14.60.16**  "He never turned his face away at the forefront of the battle, nor did he wage that grievous fight with his back to the foe.

**14.60.17**  Having slain guardians of the earth by the hundred thousand in their hosts, worn down at last by Droṇa and Karṇa, he fell into the power of Duḥśāsana's son.

**14.60.18**  For so long as he fought one against one, O lord, he could not have been slain in battle even by the wielder of the thunderbolt.

**14.60.19**  But when Pārtha had been drawn off into combat by the sworn warriors, the boy was hemmed in by Droṇa and the rest, all furious, in that battle.

**14.60.20**  Then, having wrought a very great slaughter of his father's foes in battle, your daughter's son, O Vārṣṇeya, fell into the power of Duḥśāsana's son.

**14.60.21**  Surely he has gone to heaven; cast off your grief, O wise one. Good men, meeting with calamity, never sink down into despair.

**14.60.22**  He by whom Droṇa, Karṇa and the rest, peers of great Indra, were met in battle—how should he not attain heaven?

**14.60.23**  Cast off your grief, O unassailable one, and do not yield to anguish; for that conqueror of hostile cities has won the goal hallowed by the sword."

**14.60.24**  But when that hero was slain, this Subhadrā, my sister, stricken with grief, came to Pṛthā and shrieked aloud like an osprey.

**14.60.25**  And drawing near to Draupadī, she asked in her grief: "Noble lady, where are all our boys? I long to look upon them."

**14.60.26**  Hearing her words, all those women of the Kurus took her in their arms and wailed in utmost anguish.

**14.60.27**  And Bhadrā said to Uttarā: "Gentle one, where has your husband gone? Tell him to come to me quickly."

**14.60.28**  "Surely, daughter of Virāṭa, in former days, on hearing my voice, he would rush forth at once from the house. Why does your lord not come to you now?"

**14.60.29**  "O Abhimanyu, your uncles, the great chariot-warriors, are well; and all of them have asked after your welfare, you who came here eager for battle."

**14.60.30**  "Tell me now of the battle as before, O subduer of foes. Why do you not answer me here today as I lament?"

**14.60.31**  Hearing this and the rest of the lament of the Vṛṣṇi lady, Pṛthā, sore stricken with grief, then slowly spoke these words:

**14.60.32**  "Subhadrā, the boy who was guarded in battle by Vāsudeva, by Sātyaki, and by his own father, was slain by the law of Time.

**14.60.33**  Such is the law of mortals; grieve not, O delight of the Yadus. For your unconquerable son has attained the supreme goal.

**14.60.34**  You are born in a great house of high-souled warriors; grieve not for the restless-eyed boy, O you whose eyes are like the lotus.

**14.60.35**  Look to Uttarā, who is with child; grieve not, O lovely one. For this fair lady will soon bring forth his son."

**14.60.36**  Having thus comforted her, O upholder of the Yadu line, Kuntī cast off her hard-to-bear grief and made ready his funeral rites.

**14.60.37**  With the leave of the king, of Bhīma too, and of the twins who were the peers of Yama, the knower of dharma bestowed gifts in great abundance.

**14.60.38**  Then, having given many cows to the brāhmaṇas, O upholder of the Yadus, the Vṛṣṇi lady grew calmer and said this to the daughter of Virāṭa:

**14.60.39**  "Daughter of Virāṭa, you must not grieve here for your husband, O glorious one. Fair-hipped lady, guard for me the child within your womb."

**14.60.40**  Having spoken thus, O most resplendent one, Kuntī fell silent; and taking leave of her, I brought this Subhadrā away.

**14.60.41**  Thus did your daughter's son meet his end, O Mādhava. Cast off your anguish, O unassailable one, and set not your mind upon grief."

**14.61.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Hearing these words of his son, the righteous son of Śūra then cast off his grief and performed a funeral rite beyond compare.

**14.61.2**  Vāsudeva likewise, for the dear and high-souled son of his sister, duly performed the rites for the departed.

**14.61.3**  The mighty-armed one feasted sixty hundred-thousands of brāhmaṇas, after due rule, with food endowed with every excellence.

**14.61.4**  And the mighty-armed Kṛṣṇa, having clothed the brāhmaṇas, drove away their thirst for wealth; and that was a thing to make the hair stand on end.

**14.61.5**  Gold, cows, beds and coverings—as these were being given, the brāhmaṇas then cried out, "It is enough, it is more than enough!"

**14.61.6**  Then Vāsudeva the Dāśārha, Baladeva with Sātyaki, and Satyaka performed the funeral rite of Abhimanyu; sorely consumed with grief, they could find no peace.

**14.61.7**  In the same way the heroic Pāṇḍavas, in the city named for the elephant, found no peace, bereft as they were of Abhimanyu.

**14.61.8**  And for very many days, O lord of kings, the daughter of Virāṭa, stricken with grief for her husband, took no food—a most piteous thing—and the child within her womb began to fade away.

**14.61.9**  Then Vyāsa came, having known it by his divine sight; and arriving, the wise and mighty-splendored one said to large-eyed Pṛthā and to Uttarā: "Let this grief be cast away.

**14.61.10**  A son of great splendor shall be born to you, O glorious one, through the power of Vāsudeva and through the word I speak as well; and at the last he shall rule the earth on behalf of the Pāṇḍavas."

**14.61.11**  And looking upon Dhanaṃjaya, while the King of Righteousness watched, Vyāsa spoke these words, as though to gladden them, O Bhārata:

**14.61.12**  "A high-minded grandson shall be born to you, O mighty-armed one, and he shall rule the earth to the very bounds of the sea.

**14.61.13**  Therefore cast off your grief, O best of Kurus, O harrower of foes. There is no room for doubt in this: truly it shall come to pass for you.

**14.61.14**  And what was foretold before by the Vṛṣṇi hero Kṛṣṇa, O delight of the Kurus, shall so come to be; let there be no doubting of it in your mind.

**14.61.15**  He has gone to the imperishable worlds of the gods, won by his own deeds. He is not to be mourned by you, dear child, nor by the other Kurus either."

**14.61.16**  Thus addressed by his grandsire, the righteous Dhanaṃjaya cast off his grief, O great king, and grew glad of heart.

**14.61.17**  And your father too, O knower of dharma, O great-minded one, grew within that womb in due season, as the moon waxes in the bright fortnight.

**14.61.18**  Then Vyāsa urged the king, the son of Dharma, to undertake the horse-sacrifice; and thereupon he vanished from sight.

**14.61.19**  And the wise King of Righteousness, hearing those words of Vyāsa, set his mind, dear child, upon a journey to bring back wealth.

**14.62.1**  Janamejaya said: O brāhmaṇa, having heard these words spoken by the high-souled Vyāsa concerning the horse-sacrifice, what then did the king do?

**14.62.2**  And the treasure that was laid by Marutta beneath the surface of the earth—how did he come to win it? Tell me that, O best of the twice-born.

**14.62.3**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Having heard the words of Dvaipāyana, Yudhiṣṭhira the King of Righteousness gathered all his brothers and at the fitting time spoke—to Arjuna and Bhīmasena, and to the twin sons of Mādrī as well.

**14.62.4**  You have heard, O heroes, the counsel that out of friendship was spoken by the great-souled and wise Kṛṣṇa, who desires the welfare of the Kurus.

**14.62.5**  And by the great Vyāsa, grown mighty in austerity, wishing prosperity for his friends—our teacher, devoted to righteousness, of wondrous deeds.

**14.62.6**  And by the deeply wise Bhīṣma and the sagacious Govinda. Recalling all this, I wish to carry it out duly, O Pāṇḍavas.

**14.62.7**  For the word of those who expound the sacred truth is good for us all, both in the future and in the present, and brings blessing in its train.

**14.62.8**  This whole earth has been drained of its treasures, O upholders of the Kurus; and Vyāsa spoke at length of the wealth of Marutta, O kings.

**14.62.9**  If this seems right to you, and if you deem it fitting, let us all go to fetch it. What do you think, O Bhīma?

**14.62.10**  When the king had thus spoken, O upholder of the Kuru line, Bhīmasena, with hands joined in reverence, addressed the foremost of kings.

**14.62.11**  It pleases me, O mighty-armed one, what you have said regarding the fetching of the wealth of which Vyāsa told.

**14.62.12**  If we could but obtain that wealth of Marutta, the son of Avikṣit, O lord, then, O great king, our purpose would be as good as accomplished—such is my judgment.

**14.62.13**  By bowing down to the great-souled Lord of the Mountain, and by worshipping the matted-haired one, may we fetch it. Blessings upon you!

**14.62.14**  By propitiating that all-pervading Lord of the Lords of gods, and his attendants too, with word, thought, and deed, we shall surely gain our end.

**14.62.15**  And those servants of fearsome aspect who guard that treasure will come under our sway once the bull-bannered god is gracious.

**14.62.16**  Hearing Bhīma speak these words, O Bhārata, the king, the son of Dharma, was exceedingly pleased; and Arjuna and the others too said joyfully, So be it.

**14.62.17**  Then all the Pāṇḍavas, having resolved to bring back the treasure, commanded the army to make ready on a day and under a constellation that were steadfast and auspicious.

**14.62.18**  Then the sons of Pāṇḍu set forth, having had the brāhmaṇas pronounce blessings, and having first of all worshipped Maheśvara, the foremost of the gods.

**14.62.19**  With sweetmeats, with milk-rice, and likewise with meat-cakes they made offering, and having prayed to the great god, they set out greatly rejoicing.

**14.62.20**  As they were setting out, the foremost of the twice-born and the citizens, their hearts gladdened, uttered for them auspicious benedictions.

**14.62.21**  Then, having walked round them with reverence and bowed their heads, the sons of Pāṇḍu set forth, taking the brāhmaṇas along with the sacred fires.

**14.62.22**  Having taken leave of King Dhṛtarāṣṭra, who was stricken with grief for his sons, together with his wife, and of the large-eyed Pṛthā.

**14.62.23**  And having placed Yuyutsu, the son of Dhṛtarāṣṭra of the Kuru line, in charge at the root, they went honoured by the townsfolk and by the wise brāhmaṇas.

**14.63.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Then they set forth in high spirits, their men and mounts rejoicing, filling the earth with the mighty rumble of their chariots.

**14.63.2**  Extolled with praises by bards, heralds, and panegyrists, and encompassed by their own army, they shone like the suns wreathed in their own rays.

**14.63.3**  With a white parasol held above his head, Yudhiṣṭhira shone there like the lord of the stars, the moon, on the night of the full moon.

**14.63.4**  Along the road the Pāṇḍava, that bull among men, duly and graciously received the benedictions of victory uttered by the gladdened people.

**14.63.5**  And so too, O king, of the soldiers who followed the king, the tumultuous clamour rose up and stood spanning the very heavens.

**14.63.6**  That great king crossed over lakes and rivers, forests and groves, and at last drew near the mountain.

**14.63.7**  And in that region, O lord of kings, where that supreme treasure lay, the Pāṇḍava king made his encampment together with his soldiers, on auspicious and level ground, O best of the Bharatas.

**14.63.8**  Placing in the van the brāhmaṇas, endowed with austerity, learning, and self-restraint, and his household priest, O Kaurava, versed in the Vedas and their auxiliary sciences.

**14.63.9**  Before the encamping, the brāhmaṇas, together with the household priest, having duly performed the rites of peace, surrounded the king on every side.

**14.63.10**  And having placed the king and his ministers in the centre according to rule, the twice-born laid out the camp with six pathways and nine quarters.

**14.63.11**  And having had the station of the rutting lordly elephants prepared according to rule, that lord of kings said this to the brāhmaṇas.

**14.63.12**  In this undertaking, O best of the twice-born, on an auspicious constellation and day, as you deem fit, so you ought to act.

**14.63.13**  Let there be no waste of time for us as we tarry here. Having determined this, O foremost of brāhmaṇas, let what comes next be done.

**14.63.14**  Hearing this speech of the king, the brāhmaṇas together with the household priest, rejoicing and eager to please the King of Righteousness, spoke these words.

**14.63.15**  This very day both the constellation and the day are holy; let us strive at the most excellent of rites. Today let us dwell here on water alone, O king, and let you too keep a fast this day.

**14.63.16**  Hearing the words of those best of the twice-born, the lords of men kept the fast and passed the night, confident, upon beds of kuśa grass, like blazing sacrificial fires at the sacrifices.

**14.63.17**  Then that night passed for those great-souled ones as they listened to the words uttered by the brāhmaṇas; and at the clear dawn the bulls among the twice-born spoke to the son of Dharma, the lord of men.

**14.64.1**  The brāhmaṇas said: Let an offering be made today to the great-souled three-eyed god; and having made the offering, O king, let us then strive for our purpose.

**14.64.2**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Hearing the words of those brāhmaṇas, Yudhiṣṭhira duly presented an offering to the Lord of the Mountain.

**14.64.3**  Having gratified the fire with clarified butter duly consecrated, and having prepared the sacrificial gruel hallowed with mantras, the household priest then went forth.

**14.64.4**  Taking flowers sanctified by mantras, O lord of men, he offered the oblation with sweetmeats, with milk-rice, and with meat.

**14.64.5**  With many-coloured flowers and with parched grains of various kinds, that master of the Vedas duly performed the whole rite of the sviṣṭakṛt oblation, and thereafter made a supreme offering to the attendant servants.

**14.64.6**  To the lord of yakṣas, Kubera, and to Maṇibhadra, and likewise to the other yakṣas, and to those who are the overlords of beings.

**14.64.7**  With sesame-and-rice mixed with meat, and with funerary offerings joined with sesame, the abode of the God of gods shone exceedingly bright, O king.

**14.64.8**  Having performed worship of Rudra and of all his hosts, the king, placing Vyāsa at his head, went toward the treasure-store of jewels.

**14.64.9**  Having worshipped the Lord of Wealth, and bowed down and saluted him, with diverse flowers, with cakes, and with sesame-rice.

**14.64.10**  And having worshipped all the treasures beginning with the Śaṅkha and all the guardians of the treasures, that mighty one had the foremost brāhmaṇas pronounce their blessings.

**14.64.11**  Established in their splendour, amid the proclaiming of the holy day, that gladdened best of the Kurus had the treasure dug out.

**14.64.12**  Then there were dishes and water-pots, lovely vessels of crystal, ewers, cauldrons, jars, and beautifully shaped urns.

**14.64.13**  And many varied vessels by the thousands did Yudhiṣṭhira, the King of Righteousness, then have brought up.

**14.64.14**  Their measure was as much as a great handful; three hundred thousand such vessels there were, O king, and each weighed half a tulā, O lord.

**14.64.15**  The means of conveyance of the son of Pāṇḍu there, O lord of peoples, were sixty thousand camels, and twice a hundred thousand horses.

**14.64.16**  And elephants, O great king, numbering a hundred thousand, and wagons and chariots and as many she-elephants too; while of the asses and the men there was no reckoning.

**14.64.17**  This was the wealth that Yudhiṣṭhira raised up: sixteen, eight, and twenty-four thousand in the measure of loads.

**14.64.18**  Having loaded that treasure upon these, and having worshipped Mahādeva once more, the Pāṇḍava set out toward the city named after the elephant.

**14.64.19**  With the leave of Dvaipāyana, placing his household priest at the head, that bull among men halted at every distance of a goyuta.

**14.64.20**  That great host, O king, made its way toward the city, struggling under the burden of the wealth, and gladdening the upholders of the Kurus.

**14.65.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: At this very time, the mighty Vāsudeva too came, together with the Vṛṣṇis, to the city named after the elephant.

**14.65.2**  That bull among men, knowing the appointed time of the horse-sacrifice as the son of Dharma had told him, came while journeying toward his own city.

**14.65.3**  Accompanied by the son of Rukmiṇī, and by Yuyudhāna, by Cārudeṣṇa, Sāmba, Gada, and Kṛtavarman.

**14.65.4**  And by the hero Sāraṇa, by Niśaṭha and Ulmuka—placing Baladeva at their head, and together with Subhadrā then.

**14.65.5**  Wishing to look upon Draupadī, Uttarā, and Pṛthā, and also to console the kṣatriya women whose lords had been slain.

**14.65.6**  Seeing them arrive, King Dhṛtarāṣṭra, lord of the earth, received them duly, as did the high-minded Vidura.

**14.65.7**  There that bull among men, Kṛṣṇa of great splendour, dwelt, well honoured by Vidura and likewise by Yuyutsu.

**14.65.8**  While the Vṛṣṇi heroes were dwelling there, O Janamejaya, your father was born, O king—Parikṣit, the slayer of enemy heroes.

**14.65.9**  But that king, O great king, smitten by the brahmāstra, was born a corpse, motionless, swelling both joy and grief.

**14.65.10**  The clamour of the joyful people there, with its lion-roar, having filled all the quarters and the intermediate regions, fell silent once again.

**14.65.11**  Then Kṛṣṇa in great haste entered the inner apartments at that time, with Yuyudhāna as his second, his senses and mind shaken.

**14.65.12**  Then he saw his own father's sister coming swiftly toward him, crying out again and again to Vāsudeva, Run hither!

**14.65.13**  And behind her Draupadī, and the renowned Subhadrā, and the women of the kinsfolk, O king, crying out with lamentation and pity.

**14.65.14**  Then Kuntī, the king's daughter, having reached Kṛṣṇa, spoke to him, O tiger among kings, in a voice choked with tears.

**14.65.15**  O Vāsudeva, mighty-armed one, blessed in offspring is Devakī through you. You are our refuge and our support; this whole family depends upon you.

**14.65.16**  O foremost hero of the Yadus, this child, the son of your nephew, O lord, has been born slain by Aśvatthāman—bring him back to life, O Keśava!

**14.65.17**  For this you vowed at the hour of the reed-weapon, O delight of the Yadus: I shall revive him though born dead, O lord.

**14.65.18**  This child here has been born dead, dear one—behold him, O bull among men—and behold Uttarā and Subhadrā and Draupadī and me, O Mādhava.

**14.65.19**  And the son of Dharma, and Bhīma, and Phalguna, and Nakula, and Sahadeva too, O invincible one—you ought to save us all.

**14.65.20**  Upon this child hang the very lives of the Pāṇḍavas and my own; and on him too, O Dāśārha, depend the funeral offerings to Pāṇḍu and to my father-in-law.

**14.65.21**  Blessings on you—do today, O Janārdana, a kindness even to the departed, to the beloved Abhimanyu who was so worthy of you and so dear.

**14.65.22**  For Uttarā keeps repeating, O slayer of foes, the fond words spoken by Abhimanyu, out of her love for you, O Kṛṣṇa—of this there is no doubt.

**14.65.23**  O Dāśārha, long ago the son of Arjuna said to the daughter of Virāṭa: 'O blessed one, your son shall journey to the house of his maternal uncle.'

**14.65.24**  'Having gone to the house of the Vṛṣṇis and Andhakas, he shall acquire the science of archery, and weapons of many wondrous kinds, and the whole science of statecraft.'

**14.65.25**  Thus out of love, dear one, did the son of Subhadrā speak — that slayer of enemy heroes, that unassailable one — and so shall it surely be, beyond all doubt.

**14.65.26**  Bowing before you here, O slayer of Madhu, we beseech you: for the welfare of this lineage, work this supreme deliverance.

**14.65.27**  Having spoken thus to the Vārṣṇeya, the large-eyed Pṛthā, stricken with grief, raised up her arms, and she and the other women fell to the ground.

**14.65.28**  And, O great king, they all cried out, their eyes clouded with tears: 'The grandnephew of Vāsudeva is born dead, O lord!'

**14.65.29**  When this was spoken, Janārdana lifted up Kuntī, who had fallen to the earth, and consoled her, O Bhārata.

**14.66.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: When Pṛthā had risen, Subhadrā, beholding her brother, wailed aloud, stricken with grief, and spoke these words.

**14.66.2**  O lotus-eyed one, behold the grandson of the wise Pārtha — born wasted away among the wasted Kurus, his life already gone.

**14.66.3**  The reed-weapon hurled by the son of Droṇa, aimed against Bhīmasena, has fallen instead upon Uttarā, upon victory itself, and upon me.

**14.66.4**  That same fire stands blazing within my heart, O Keśava, since I do not see, O unassailable lord, the son of my son.

**14.66.5**  What now will the righteous Dharmarāja Yudhiṣṭhira say, and Bhīmasena and Arjuna, and those two sons of Mādrī?

**14.66.6**  When they hear that Abhimanyu's son is both born and dead, O Vārṣṇeya, the Pāṇḍavas will feel as though robbed by the son of Droṇa.

**14.66.7**  Abhimanyu was dear to his fathers, O Kṛṣṇa — of this there is no doubt. What will they say, undone by the weapon of Droṇa's son?

**14.66.8**  What grief greater than this can there be, I wonder, O Janārdana, than this — the son of Abhimanyu born dead, O Kṛṣṇa, O subduer of foes?

**14.66.9**  I, then, bow my head and entreat you today, O Kṛṣṇa — Pṛthā here too, and Draupadī. Look upon them, O best of men.

**14.66.10**  When the son of Droṇa slew the unborn children of the Pāṇḍavas, O Mādhava, then, in your wrath, you spoke to that son of Droṇa, O crusher of foes:

**14.66.11**  'Against your will I shall thwart you, O degraded Brāhmaṇa, O basest of men: I shall restore to life the son of the son of the Diademed One.'

**14.66.12**  Having heard this word of yours, and knowing your power, I entreat you, O unassailable one: let the son of Abhimanyu live.

**14.66.13**  If, having so promised, you do not make good that auspicious word, O tiger of the Vṛṣṇis, then know me to be as good as dead.

**14.66.14**  O hero, if this son of Abhimanyu does not return to life — when, O unassailable one, he could live through you — what use shall I then have for you?

**14.66.15**  Restore him to life, O unassailable one — this dead son of Abhimanyu, child of his like-eyed son, O hero — as a rain-cloud revives the parched grain by its showers.

**14.66.16**  For you, O Keśava, are righteous of soul, truthful, and true in valor; you ought, O subduer of foes, to make that word come true.

**14.66.17**  For if you so willed, you could restore to life all the dead in the three worlds; how much more this beloved newborn, the dead son of your grandnephew.

**14.66.18**  I know your power, O Kṛṣṇa, and therefore I say this to you: grant the sons of Pāṇḍu this supreme favor.

**14.66.19**  Whether as 'my sister,' O mighty-armed one, or 'she whose son is slain,' or 'she who has taken refuge in me' — by any of these you ought here to show compassion.

**14.67.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Thus addressed, O lord of kings, the slayer of Keśin, overcome with grief, declared aloud 'So be it!' — as if gladdening that gathered company.

**14.67.2**  By that word the bull among men, that mighty lord, then refreshed those people as water refreshes those tormented by heat.

**14.67.3**  Then he swiftly entered the birth-chamber of your father, O tiger among men — a chamber duly honored with white garlands.

**14.67.4**  With pots brimful of water set out on every side, with clarified butter, with firebrands of tinduka wood and with mustard seeds, O mighty-armed one,

**14.67.5**  with bright weapons laid about and fires kindled all around, and with comely old women set there for attendance — so the Unfallen One beheld it,

**14.67.6**  and surrounded by skilled and able physicians, O hero. And that glorious one saw too the demon-banishing substances of every kind, set in their proper places by knowing hands.

**14.67.7**  Seeing the birth-chamber of your father so well prepared, Hṛṣīkeśa was gladdened, and cried out, 'Well done! Well done!'

**14.67.8**  As the Vārṣṇeya spoke thus, his face all gladness, Draupadī hastened to the daughter of Virāṭa and spoke these words:

**14.67.9**  'O blessed one, here comes to you your father-in-law, the slayer of Madhu — the ancient seer, of inconceivable being, the unconquered.'

**14.67.10**  And she, restraining her tear-choked voice and her very tears, that lady drew her garments close about her, gazing upon Kṛṣṇa as upon a god.

**14.67.11**  With her heart thus burning, the suffering woman, beholding Govinda draw near, lamented piteously:

**14.67.12**  'O lotus-eyed one, look upon these two children left bereft — Abhimanyu and myself — slain alike, O Janārdana.'

**14.67.13**  'O Vārṣṇeya, O slayer of Madhu, O hero, I bow my head and entreat you: bring back to life this son of mine, scorched by the weapon of Droṇa's son.'

**14.67.14**  'If by the Dharmarāja, or by Bhīmasena, or by you, O lotus-eyed one, such a word as this had been spoken —'

**14.67.15**  '"Let this reed-weapon slay the unknowing mother," O lord — then I myself would have perished, and matters would not have come to this pass.'

**14.67.16**  'By the cruel deed of felling this child in the womb with the Brahmā-weapon, what fruit does that evil-minded son of Droṇa reap?'

**14.67.17**  'I bow my head and entreat you, O destroyer of foes: I shall give up my life, O Govinda, if this child does not return to life.'

**14.67.18**  'For all the many hopes that were mine, O noble one, vested in this child, have been slain by the son of Droṇa. Why then should I live, O Keśava?'

**14.67.19**  'It was my thought, O Kṛṣṇa, my lap full at last, O Janārdana: "I shall make him salute you and rejoice, by fortune's grace" — and that has now been made vain.'

**14.67.20**  'Now that this heir of the restless-eyed one lies dead, O bull among men, all the hopes within my heart, O Kṛṣṇa, are made fruitless.'

**14.67.21**  'The restless-eyed one was, they say, exceedingly dear to you, O slayer of Madhu: look upon this his son, struck down by the Brahmā-weapon.'

**14.67.22**  'Ungrateful is this child, and cruel, even as his begetter was — he who, forsaking the Pāṇḍava fortune, has gone today to the abode of Yama.'

**14.67.23**  'And this I vowed at the forefront of battle, O Keśava: "When Abhimanyu is slain, O hero, I shall follow you ere long."'

**14.67.24**  'And that I did not do, O Kṛṣṇa — cruel, and clinging to life. What now will the son of Phalguna say to me when I come to him there?'

**14.68.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Having thus piteously lamented, the suffering Uttarā, as though distraught, fell wretchedly to the ground, yearning for her son.

**14.68.2**  Beholding her fallen, stripped of kin and of all that adorned her, Kuntī cried out, stricken with grief, and all the women of the Bharatas with her.

**14.68.3**  For a while, O king, the dwelling of the Pāṇḍavas became a thing one could not bear to look upon, resounding with cries of anguish.

**14.68.4**  And for a while, O lord of kings, the daughter of Virāṭa, tormented by grief for her son, lay overcome by a swoon, O hero.

**14.68.5**  But when Uttarā had regained her senses, O bull of the Bharatas, she set that son upon her lap and spoke these words:

**14.68.6**  'Being the son of one who knew righteousness, do you yet take to unrighteousness — you who offer no salutation to the foremost hero of the Vṛṣṇis?'

**14.68.7**  'O son, going to your father, speak my word to him: "Hard is it, O hero, for living beings to die in any way until their appointed time has come."'

**14.68.8**  'For I, bereft today of you — both husband and son — though death were fitting, yet live on, robbed of all blessing, possessed of nothing.'

**14.68.9**  'Or, with the leave of the Dharmarāja, O mighty-armed one, I shall swallow a sharp poison, or enter the devouring fire.'

**14.68.10**  'Yet hard it is to die, my dear, since this heart of mine, bereft of husband and son, does not burst asunder into a thousand pieces.'

**14.68.11**  'Rise, my son, and look upon this grieving great-grandmother — anguished, overwhelmed, forlorn, sunk in an ocean of sorrow.'

**14.68.12**  'Behold too the noble Pāñcālī, and the suffering daughter of the Sātvatas, and behold me, sore stricken with grief, like a doe pierced by the hunter.'

**14.68.13**  'Rise, and behold the face of the wise Lord of the worlds, its eyes like lotus-petals, restless-glancing as of old.'

**14.68.14**  Seeing Uttarā thus lamenting and again fallen, all those women once more raised her up.

**14.68.15**  Then, recovering her composure and rising, the daughter of the Matsya king, with hands joined in reverence, saluted the lotus-eyed one upon the very ground.

**14.68.16**  Having heard her abundant lament, that bull among men, Kṛṣṇa, sipped water in purification and then withdrew that Brahmā-weapon.

**14.68.17**  And the Dāśārha, the Unfallen One, vowed the child's life, and, pure of soul, he spoke, making all the world to hear:

**14.68.18**  'I do not speak falsely, O Uttarā: this shall come true. I do here restore this child to life before the eyes of all embodied beings.'

**14.68.19**  'Never before have I spoken falsely, not even in jest, nor have I ever turned my back in battle: by that truth, let this child live.'

**14.68.20**  'As righteousness is dear to me, and the Brāhmaṇas above all — by that truth, let this son of Abhimanyu, born dead, live again.'

**14.68.21**  'As I have never known any quarrel with Vijaya at any time — by that truth, let this dead infant live.'

**14.68.22**  'As truth and righteousness are ever established in me — even so, let this dead infant, the son of Abhimanyu, live.'

**14.68.23**  'As Kaṃsa and Keśin were slain by me through righteousness — by that truth, let this child here come back again to life.'

**14.68.24**  Thus spoken over by Vāsudeva, that child, O bull of the Bharatas, little by little, O great king, began to stir, returning to consciousness.

**14.69.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: When the Brahmā-weapon, O king, was withdrawn by Kṛṣṇa, then that chamber was lit up with the splendor of your father.

**14.69.2**  Then all the demons, forsaking that house, fled away, and a voice came from the sky: 'Well done, O Keśava, well done!'

**14.69.3**  And that blazing weapon then departed to the Grandfather, and thereupon your father regained his breath, O lord of men, and that child stirred according to his vigor and strength.

**14.69.4**  Then the women of the Bharatas were filled with joy, O king, and at Govinda's command they had the Brāhmaṇas recite their blessings.

**14.69.5**  Then all those women, rejoicing, praised Janārdana — the women of the lions among the Bharatas — like folk who reach the far shore on finding a boat.

**14.69.6**  Kuntī, and the daughter of Drupada, and Subhadrā, and Uttarā, and the other women of those lions among men became glad of heart.

**14.69.7**  There the wrestlers, the actors, the boxers, the reciters of legends, and those skilled in pleasant attendance, together with the throngs of bards and panegyrists, praised Janārdana, O bull of the Bharatas, blessing your house, the line of Kuru, with the recital of its glories.

**14.69.8**  Rising at the proper hour, Uttarā, glad at heart, saluted the joy of the Yadus together with her son, O Bhārata; and well pleased, he gave to her in particular abundant jewels.

**14.69.9**  And so did the other tigers of the Vṛṣṇis; and the lord Janārdana, faithful to his word, conferred a name upon your father, O great king.

**14.69.10**  "Since this son of Abhimanyu has been born when the lineage had dwindled away, let his name be Parikṣit," he said then.

**14.69.11**  In due season your father grew, O ruler of men, and he was a delight to the hearts of all the world, O Bhārata.

**14.69.12**  When your father was a month old, O hero, O Bhārata, the Pāṇḍavas arrived, bringing very great store of jewels.

**14.69.13**  Hearing that they had drawn near, the foremost of the Vṛṣṇis went forth to meet them, and the men adorned Hāstinapura with masses of garlands.

**14.69.14**  With many-coloured banners and with manifold flags the townsfolk too adorned their houses, O ruler of the people.

**14.69.15**  And worship of many kinds at the temples of the gods Vidura ordained, out of his wish to please the sons of Pāṇḍu.

**14.69.16**  The royal highways there were decked with flowers, and the city shone, resounding like the surging of the ocean.

**14.69.17**  With dancers performing their dances and with the singing of the minstrels, that city then was like the dwelling of Vaiśravaṇa.

**14.69.18**  With its bards and men by thousands, accompanied by women, it was made beautiful on every side, here and there in its secluded places, O king.

**14.69.19**  The banners, shaken by the breathing wind, seemed then as if to point out the Kurus to the south and to the north.

**14.69.20**  And a man set at the head of the royal proclamation cried aloud then: "This day there shall be revelry all night long, marked by jewels and ornaments!"

**14.70.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Hearing that the Pāṇḍavas had drawn near, Vāsudeva, the harrower of foes, went forth with his ministers, desiring to see them.

**14.70.2**  Having met together in due form, the Pāṇḍavas along with the Vṛṣṇis entered, all united, the city named after the elephant, O king.

**14.70.3**  And by the sound of the hooves and the chariot-wheels of that great host, heaven and earth and the sky were enveloped in the din.

**14.70.4**  Setting their treasure at the fore, the Pāṇḍavas then entered their own city, glad at heart, with their ministers and the bands of their friends.

**14.70.5**  Approaching King Dhṛtarāṣṭra in due form, and proclaiming each his own name, they reverenced his feet.

**14.70.6**  And after Dhṛtarāṣṭra those best of the Bharatas then saluted Gāndhārī, the daughter of Subala, and Kuntī, O tiger among kings.

**14.70.7**  And having honoured Vidura, and met with the son born of the Vaiśya woman, those heroes, themselves being honoured, shone forth, O lord of the people.

**14.70.8**  Then those heroes heard of that supreme wonder, strange and great and marvellous—the birth of your father, O Bhārata.

**14.70.9**  Hearing of that deed of the wise Vāsudeva, they honoured, as one worthy of honour, Kṛṣṇa, the joy of Devakī.

**14.70.10**  Then, after a few days, Vyāsa the son of Satyavatī, of great splendour, came to the city named after the elephant.

**14.70.11**  To him all the upholders of the Kurus did honour in due form, and together with the tigers of the Vṛṣṇis and Andhakas they then waited upon him.

**14.70.12**  There, when tales of many sorts had been recounted, Yudhiṣṭhira the son of Dharma spoke these words to Vyāsa:

**14.70.13**  "By your grace, O blessed one, this treasure has been gathered; I wish to employ it in the great rite of the horse sacrifice.

**14.70.14**  For this I desire your sanction, O best of sages; we are all dependent upon you and upon the great-souled Kṛṣṇa."

**14.70.15**  Vyāsa said: "I grant you leave, O king; let what follows be done. Sacrifice with a horse sacrifice, performed according to rule and rich in priestly gifts.

**14.70.16**  For the horse sacrifice, O lord of kings, is a purifier from all sins; having sacrificed therewith you shall surely be freed of sin—of this there is no doubt."

**14.70.17**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Thus addressed, the righteous Yudhiṣṭhira, king of the Kurus, O Kaurava, set his mind upon the undertaking of the horse sacrifice.

**14.70.18**  Having taken leave of Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana, the eloquent king then addressed Vāsudeva and spoke these words:

**14.70.19**  "Blessed in her offspring is the lady Devakī through you, O best of men; whatever I may ask of you, O mighty-armed, do that here, O Acyuta.

**14.70.20**  It is enjoyments won by your power that we enjoy, O joy of the Yadus; by your valour and your wisdom this earth has been conquered.

**14.70.21**  Consecrate yourself for the rite; you are our highest teacher. When you have offered the sacrifice, O knower of dharma, I shall be freed of sin, O lord; for you are the whole imperishable sacrifice, you are dharma, you are Prajāpati."

**14.70.22**  Vāsudeva said: "It is fitting, O mighty-armed, that you yourself should say this, O subduer of foes; that you are the refuge of all beings—such is my settled conviction.

**14.70.23**  And you today shine forth among the Kuru heroes by your righteousness; we are but your dependents, O king; you, O king, are deemed by us our teacher.

**14.70.24**  Sacrifice with my sanction; the rite is as good as performed by me. Let your honour set us to the task wherein you desire it, O Bhārata; truly I promise you, I shall do all for you, O sinless one.

**14.70.25**  Bhīmasena and Arjuna, and likewise the two sons of Mādrī, shall be deemed to have sacrificed when you have sacrificed, O Bhārata."

**14.71.1**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Thus addressed by Kṛṣṇa, the wise Yudhiṣṭhira, son of Dharma, turned to Vyāsa and then spoke these words:

**14.71.2**  "As you know truly the proper time for the horse sacrifice, then consecrate me; for upon you depends my rite."

**14.71.3**  Vyāsa said: "I, and Paila, O son of Kuntī, and likewise Yājñavalkya—we shall be the performers of the ordinance according to its proper times, of this there is no doubt.

**14.71.4**  On the full-moon day of Caitra your consecration shall take place; let the materials be gathered for the sake of the sacrifice, O bull among men.

**14.71.5**  And let the charioteers skilled in the lore of horses, and the brāhmaṇas versed in it, examine a horse fit for the rite, for the accomplishment of your sacrifice's purpose.

**14.71.6**  Releasing it according to the scriptures, let it roam over the earth that is girdled by the sea, increasing your glory and your name, O king."

**14.71.7**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Thus addressed, the Pāṇḍava lord of the earth said "So be it" and did everything, O lord of kings, as the expounder of Brahman had said; and all the materials, O lord of kings, were made ready.

**14.71.8**  Having gathered the materials, the king, the son of Dharma, of immeasurable soul, then made his report to Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana.

**14.71.9**  Then Vyāsa of great splendour said to the king, the son of Dharma: "At the proper time and in fitting manner we are ready for your consecration.

**14.71.10**  Let the sacrificial ladle and the bundle of grass be of gold, O Kaurava, and whatever else there may be fit for the rite, let that be made of gold.

**14.71.11**  And let the horse be released today upon the earth in due order, and let it roam well-guarded according to the scriptures, O Yudhiṣṭhira."

**14.71.12**  Yudhiṣṭhira said: "This horse, O brāhmaṇa, has been released by me; it shall roam this earth at its will. Therein let provision be made.

**14.71.13**  Who should guard the horse as it wanders the earth, moving at its own pleasure—of this, O sage, your honour should speak."

**14.71.14**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Thus addressed, O lord of kings, Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana replied: "He who is younger than Bhīmasena, the foremost of all bowmen,
