**14.91.8**  Having accepted the earth, O king, Vyāsa the son of Satyavatī addressed the best of the Bharatas, the righteous-souled Yudhiṣṭhira:

**14.91.9**  "This earth, O best of kings, which you have bestowed, is yours again; let a redemption-price be given to me instead, for brāhmaṇas are seekers of wealth."

**14.91.10**  Then the great-minded and wise Yudhiṣṭhira, in the midst of high-souled kings and together with his brothers, replied to those brāhmaṇas:

**14.91.11**  "In the great horse-sacrifice the earth is declared to be the priestly fee; this earth, won by Arjuna, has been delivered by me to the officiating priests.

**14.91.12**  I shall enter the forest, O lords among brāhmaṇas; divide this earth among yourselves, making the earth into four parts according to the measure of the four classes of priests.

**14.91.13**  I have no wish to take back what is now the brāhmaṇas' wealth, O best of sages; this is ever my judgement and that of my brothers, O sinless ones."

**14.91.14**  When he had spoken thus, his brothers and Draupadī said, "So be it indeed"; and that was a thing that made the hair stand on end.

**14.91.15**  Then there came a voice from the sky, O Bhārata, crying "Well done, well done!"; and likewise rose the sound of the assembled brāhmaṇas in praise.

**14.91.16**  Dvaipāyana, thus addressed, spoke again to Yudhiṣṭhira; in the midst of the brāhmaṇas the sage said this, honouring him:

**14.91.17**  "This earth has been given by you to me; I give it back to you. Let gold be given to these twice-born ones, but let the earth be yours."

**14.91.18**  Then Vāsudeva said to the Dharma-king Yudhiṣṭhira: "As the blessed Vyāsa has spoken, so you ought to do."

**14.91.19**  Thus addressed, that best of the Kurus, glad of heart, together with his brothers gave as the sacrificial fee threefold crores upon crores.

**14.91.20**  No other king in the world will do what was done by that lion of the Kurus, who was emulating Marutta.

**14.91.21**  Having accepted that wealth, the mighty and learned Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana gave it to the officiating priests, and they divided it into four parts.

**14.91.22**  Having given that gold as the redemption-price of the earth, Yudhiṣṭhira, his sins shaken off and heaven won, rejoiced together with his brothers.

**14.91.23**  The officiating priests then distributed that boundless heap of gold among the twice-born, according to each one's zeal and according to his strength.

**14.91.24**  And whatever gold or ornament there was in the sacrificial enclosure — the archways, the posts, the pots, the vessels, and the bricks — all of it the brāhmaṇas, with Yudhiṣṭhira's leave, divided among themselves.

**14.91.25**  After the brāhmaṇas, the kṣatriyas took away wealth, and so too the throngs of vaiśyas and śūdras, and others of the barbarian tribes; over a long time they carried off that gold from here and there.

**14.91.26**  Then all those brāhmaṇas, gladdened and satisfied with that wealth by the great-souled Dharma-king, went away to their dwellings.

**14.91.27**  The blessed Vyāsa of great lustre, in return for the homage paid to his feet, gave his own share of that great store of gold to Kuntī.

**14.91.28**  Having received that gift of affection from her father-in-law, Pṛthā, glad of heart, then performed a very great act of merit in the world.

**14.91.29**  Having gone to the concluding ablution, the king, freed of sin and together with his brothers, shone forth, honoured all around, like great Indra among the gods.

**14.91.30**  And the Pāṇḍavas, surrounded then by assembled kings, shone, O great king, like the planets amid the host of stars.

**14.91.31**  Then to the kings as well he gave jewels of many kinds, elephants, horses, ornaments, women, garments, and gold.

**14.91.32**  Pouring out that boundless flood of wealth upon the circle of kings, the royal son of Pṛthā shone as does Vaiśravaṇa, the lord of riches.

**14.91.33**  And having summoned the heroic king Babhruvāhana and bestowed on him abundant wealth, he then sent him home.

**14.91.34**  And, O bull among kings, he lovingly consecrated the young grandson of Duḥśalā in his own kingdom, which had been guarded by his forefathers.

**14.91.35**  And the self-controlled Yudhiṣṭhira, king of the Kurus, having well rewarded and duly honoured all those kings, sent them on their way.

**14.91.36**  Thus was that sacrifice of the wise Dharma-king — a flood of plentiful food, wealth, and jewels, an ocean of wine and spirituous liquor.

**14.91.37**  There, O bull of the Bharatas, were lakes whose mire was clarified butter and many mountains of food, and streams whose mud was sweet rasālā.

**14.91.38**  "Let dainties and condiments and relishes be prepared! Let them be eaten!" — and of the beasts being slaughtered no end could there be seen.

**14.91.39**  Joyous with people drunk and rapt with delight, with young women singing, and with the sounds of drum and conch, the place was then a thing to charm the heart.

**14.91.40**  "Let it be given! Let it be eaten!" — thus, by day and night and without restraint, men dwelling in many lands told of that scene, like a great festival, thronged with people in the highest joy.

**14.91.41**  Having rained down showers of wealth — of pleasures, jewels, and riches — the best of the Bharatas, freed of sin and his purpose fulfilled, entered the city.

**14.92.1**  Janamejaya said: Whatever marvel there was in the sacrifice of my grandsire, the wise son of Dharma, that you ought to tell me.

**14.92.2**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Hear, O tiger among kings, of the great and surpassing marvel that came to pass, O mighty one, when the great horse-sacrifice was concluded.

**14.92.3**  When the foremost brāhmaṇas had been satisfied, and the kinsmen, relations, and friends, and the poor, the blind, and the wretched too, O best of the Bharatas —

**14.92.4**  while the great gift was being proclaimed in all the quarters, O Bhārata, and showers of flowers were falling upon the head of the Dharma-king —

**14.92.5**  a mongoose with one side of gold came forth from its hole, O sinless one, and uttered a cry like the crash of the thunderbolt, O lord of peoples.

**14.92.6**  Having once sent forth that cry and terrified the beasts and birds, the great, bold dweller in the hole spoke in human speech.

**14.92.7**  "This sacrifice of yours, O kings, is not equal to the prastha-measure of barley-meal given by a generous man who lived by gleaning, dwelling at Kurukṣetra."

**14.92.8**  Having heard that speech of the mongoose, O lord of peoples, all those bulls among brāhmaṇas were struck with the utmost amazement.

**14.92.9**  Then those brāhmaṇas, gathering round the mongoose, questioned it: "Whence have you come to this sacrifice, this assembly of the good?

**14.92.10**  What is your supreme strength, what your learning, what your refuge? How are we to know you, who disparage our sacrifice —

**14.92.11**  a sacrifice performed by priests who know the rules, without violating the whole of sacred tradition, according to tradition and according to law, and the duty done as it should be done?

**14.92.12**  Here those worthy of honour were honoured duly, with the eye of scripture; the fire was kindled with offerings purified by sacred verses; what was to be given was given without grudging.

**14.92.13**  And here the bulls among the twice-born were satisfied by gifts of many kinds, the kṣatriyas by good warfare, and the ancestors by the funeral offerings;

**14.92.14**  the vaiśyas were satisfied by protection, the noble women by the granting of their desires, the śūdras by acts of compassion, and the common folk by the remnants of the gifts.

**14.92.15**  The kinsmen and relations were satisfied by the purity of our king, the gods by the holy oblations, and those who had come for shelter by his protection.

**14.92.16**  Tell us what is true in this matter, O you who are true to your word among the twice-born, as you have heard it and as you have seen it, being asked out of regard for the brāhmaṇas.

**14.92.17**  Your speech is worthy of trust, and you are wise; you bear a divine form, and you have come among the brāhmaṇas; you ought therefore to speak the truth."

**14.92.18**  Thus questioned by those twice-born, the mongoose laughed and said: "No false word have I spoken, O brāhmaṇas, nor anything said out of arrogance."

**14.92.19**  "What little I have said, and what you too have heard from me — that this sacrifice of yours, O kings, is not equal to a measure of barley-meal given by a generous gleaner who dwelt in Kurukṣetra —

**14.92.20**  "this I must surely tell you, O bulls among the twice-born. Listen with undistracted minds as I relate it, O foremost of brāhmaṇas."

**14.92.21**  "It is the supreme marvel that I myself experienced and witnessed — what befell that gleaner who dwelt in Kurukṣetra,

**14.92.22**  "by which deed a brāhmaṇa attained heaven together with his wife, his son and his daughter-in-law, and by which this half of my body here was turned to gold."

**14.93.1**  The mongoose said: "Come then, I shall recount to you the supreme fruit of a gift — a slender gift, righteously obtained and bestowed upon a brāhmaṇa, O twice-born ones."

**14.93.2**  "In Kurukṣetra, the field of righteousness, peopled by many who knew the law, there once lived a certain brāhmaṇa who subsisted by gleaning, living after the manner of the pigeon."

**14.93.3**  "With his wife, his son, and his daughter-in-law, he abode in austerity — a household of four, his daughter-in-law the fourth; aged, righteous of soul, his senses subdued."

**14.93.4**  "That brāhmaṇa of firm vows would eat with them only at the sixth mealtime; and sometimes even at the sixth mealtime no food was to be had, so that the best of the twice-born would eat only at the next sixth mealtime."

**14.93.5**  "When a dreadful famine came upon him who lived by the pigeon's law, there was then no store laid by, O brāhmaṇas — mark this well. The stock of herbs was spent, and he was left without any provision."

**14.93.6**  "As each mealtime came round, no food was to be found; and all of them, gripped by hunger, set out to glean."

**14.93.7**  "Gleaning in the bright fortnight while the sun blazed at midday, that brāhmaṇa, steadfast in austerity, tormented by heat and by hunger, gained no gleanings at all, he together with his household."

**14.93.8**  "Even thus possessed by hunger, the best of the twice-born, his life hanging by a thread, touched water according to the rite and so let that mealtime pass."

**14.93.9**  "Then, when the sixth mealtime had come, he earned a prastha-measure of barley; and from that prastha of barley those ascetics made meal."

**14.93.10**  "Having performed their daily prayers and offered to the fire according to the rite, those ascetics divided it all, a kuḍava-measure for each."

**14.93.11**  "Then, as they were about to eat, a certain brāhmaṇa came as a guest; and seeing the guest there, they grew glad of heart."

**14.93.12**  "They greeted him and asked after his welfare — pure of mind, self-controlled, endowed with faith and restraint,

**14.93.13**  "free of envy, free of anger, good men free of malice, having renounced pride, having conquered wrath — those excellent brāhmaṇas who knew the law."

**14.93.14**  "Having declared to one another their lineage and their course of sacred study, they ushered the hunger-stricken guest into their hut."

**14.93.15**  "'Here is water for your hands and water for your feet, and here is a seat of grass for you, O sinless one; and here is pure barley-meal, won by our observances, O lord. Accept it — blessings upon you — it is given by me, O best of the twice-born.'"

**14.93.16**  "Thus addressed, the brāhmaṇa accepted that kuḍava of meal and ate it, O lord of kings; yet he did not attain satisfaction."

**14.93.17**  "The gleaner, observing that the brāhmaṇa was still gripped by hunger, fell to pondering about food: 'How might he be made content?'"

**14.93.18**  "His wife said to him, O king: 'Let my portion be given. Let this best of the twice-born depart contented, as he wishes.'"

**14.93.19**  "But that righteous bull among brāhmaṇas, knowing that the virtuous woman who spoke thus was herself gripped by hunger, would not approve the giving of that meal."

**14.93.20**  "Knowing his wife to be aged, hunger-stricken, weary, faint, an ascetic woman reduced to skin and bone and trembling, he then said to her:"

**14.93.21**  "'Even among insects and winged creatures and beasts, O lovely one, the females are to be protected and nourished. You ought not to speak in this wise.'"

**14.93.22**  "'A man who is shown compassion by a woman, nourished and protected by her, would fall from his blazing renown and would not attain the higher worlds.'"

**14.93.23**  "Thus addressed, she then said: 'Our merit and our purpose are alike, O brāhmaṇa. Take this quarter-part of the prastha of meal; be gracious to me.'"

**14.93.24**  "'Truth, delight, righteousness, and heaven won by virtue — all that women desire depends upon the husband, O best of the twice-born.'"

**14.93.25**  "'The season belongs to the mother, the seed to the father; the husband is the supreme deity. By the husband's grace come to women both delight and the fruit that is offspring.'"

**14.93.26**  "'Since you protect me, you are my husband; since you maintain me, you are my support; and since you have given me a son, you are my bestower of boons. Therefore take my meal.'"

**14.93.27**  "'You too are overtaken by age and old, hunger-stricken and grievously weak, worn out by fasting and wasted away.'"

**14.93.28**  "Thus addressed by her, he took the meal and spoke these words: 'O brāhmaṇa, accept this meal as well, O excellent one.'"

**14.93.29**  "The brāhmaṇa took it and ate it, yet attained no satisfaction; and the gleaner, marking this, fell once more into anxious thought."

**14.93.30**  "The son said: 'Take this meal of mine and give it to the brāhmaṇa, O excellent father. This I deem a meritorious deed, and therefore I do it.'"

**14.93.31**  "'For you are to be cherished by me with every effort, O best of the twice-born; this is the desire of good men — the sustaining of an aged father.'"

**14.93.32**  "'For this is the very purpose of a son — protection in old age. This is the teaching, O sage among brāhmaṇas, renowned throughout the three worlds.'"

**14.93.33**  "'By merely keeping your life-breath, you can perform austerities; for the life-breath is the supreme duty, abiding in the bodies of embodied beings.'"

**14.93.34**  "The father said: 'Even were you a thousand years old, you would still be reckoned a child by me; for a father, having begotten a son, becomes one who has fulfilled his task.'"

**14.93.35**  "'The hunger of the young is strong — this I know, my dear one. I am old, and I shall endure it. Keep your strength, my son.'"

**14.93.36**  "'With my years grown decayed, my son, hunger does not even afflict me; long have I practised austerity, and I have no fear of death.'"

**14.93.37**  "The son said: 'I am your offspring, your son; and a son is so named because he saves. The son is held to be one's very self; therefore by your self save your self here.'"

**14.93.38**  "The father said: 'In form you are like me, and in character and in self-restraint; and I have tested you many times. Therefore I will take your meal.'"

**14.93.39**  "So saying, that best of the twice-born, glad of soul, took the meal and, almost smiling, gave it then to the brāhmaṇa."

**14.93.40**  "Yet even after eating that meal he was not satisfied; and the gleaner, that excellent brāhmaṇa, was overcome with shame."

**14.93.41**  "Then the virtuous daughter-in-law, standing by, desiring to please the brāhmaṇa, gladly took up her meal and spoke this word to her elder:"

**14.93.42**  "'Through your line my line shall continue, O brāhmaṇa. Take this meal of mine and give it to the guest.'"

**14.93.43**  "'By the continuance of your progeny, imperishable worlds will surely be mine; through a grandson one attains those worlds, going whither one grieves no more.'"

**14.93.44**  "'As the triad headed by righteousness, and likewise the triad of sacred fires, so too the triad of sons and grandsons is, in heaven, surely imperishable.'"

**14.93.45**  "'A son delivers the ancestors because he saves them — so we have heard. And through sons and grandsons one assuredly enjoys the worlds of the righteous.'"

**14.93.46**  "The father-in-law said: 'Seeing you with your limbs wasted by wind and sun, pale and emaciated, O you of pious conduct, your mind distraught with hunger —'"

**14.93.47**  "'how shall I take your meal and so become a destroyer of righteousness? O auspicious one of blessed conduct, you ought not to speak thus.'"

**14.93.48**  "'You who keep your vow until the sixth mealtime, endowed with good conduct and purity, living a hard life, without food — how could I bear to look upon you so?'"

**14.93.49**  "'Young, hunger-stricken, and a woman, you are ever to be protected by me; for you, worn out by fasting, are the delight of our kin.'"

**14.93.50**  "The daughter-in-law said: 'You are the elder of my elder, the deity of deities, the god beyond gods; therefore take my meal, O lord.'"

**14.93.51**  "'My body, my life-breath, and my merit — all this is for the service of my elder. By your grace, O brāhmaṇa, I shall attain the worlds I long for.'"

**14.93.52**  "'Thinking either "she is to be regarded" or "she is of firm devotion," or else "she is one for whom I must provide," you ought, O brāhmaṇa, to take this meal.'"

**14.93.53**  "The father-in-law said: 'By this ever-virtuous conduct of yours you shine, O good woman — you who, endowed with righteous vows, look to the service of your elders.'"

**14.93.54**  "'Therefore I will take your meal, O daughter-in-law; you do not deserve to be cheated of your due. Reckoned among them, O blessed one, you are the foremost of the upholders of righteousness.'"

**14.93.55**  "So saying, he took up that meal and gave it to the brāhmaṇa; and then that brāhmaṇa was at last contented by the gift of that great-souled and righteous man."

**14.93.56**  "Glad of soul, that eloquent best of the twice-born — who was Dharma himself in human form — then spoke this word to the bull among brāhmaṇas:"

**14.93.57**  "'By your pure gift, righteously obtained with effort and given freely to the best of your power, I am well pleased, O excellent brāhmaṇa.'"

**14.93.58**  "'Behold! Your gift is being proclaimed in heaven by the dwellers there; and see the rain of flowers falling from the sky upon the earth.'"

**14.93.59**  "'The divine sages, gods, and gandharvas, and those who go before the gods, and the messengers of the gods, stand here singing your praises, amazed at your gift.'"

**14.93.60**  "'The brahmic sages seated in their celestial cars, and those who have gone to the world of Brahmā, long to behold you. Go to heaven, O bull among brāhmaṇas.'"

**14.93.61**  "'All your ancestors who have gone to the world of the fathers are delivered by you, and many more who are yet to come, through many ages hence.'"

**14.93.62**  "'By chastity, by sacrifice, by giving, and likewise by austerity, and by guileless righteousness — therefore go to heaven, O brāhmaṇa.'"

**14.93.63**  "'Because you, O man of firm vows, practise austerity with supreme faith, the gods are pleased with you for this, O most excellent of brāhmaṇas.'"

**14.93.64**  "'Since all this, your every possession, was given up with a pure heart in a time of dire need, by that deed of yours this heaven has been won.'"

**14.93.65**  "'Hunger drives away wisdom and dispels the righteous mind; and one whose understanding is gripped by hunger forsakes even his firmness.'"

**14.93.66**  "'But whoever conquers the craving to eat surely conquers heaven; and when one delights in giving, then righteousness does not falter.'"

**14.93.67**  "'Disregarding love for your son and love for your wife, and knowing righteousness alone to be your true elder, you reckoned not your own craving.'"

**14.93.68**  "'Slender is the acquiring of goods by men; greater than that is a gift to a worthy recipient; greater than the gift is the right time; and greater still than that is faith.'"

**14.93.69**  "'The gate of heaven is exceeding subtle, and through delusion men do not see it. The bolt that bars heaven is the seed of greed, hidden by passion, hard to overcome.'"

**14.93.70**  "'But those men perceive that gate who have conquered anger and subdued their senses — brāhmaṇas yoked to austerity, who give according to their power.'"

**14.93.71**  "'He who can give a thousand and gives a hundred, he who can give a hundred and gives ten, and he who gives but water according to his power — all are held to reap an equal fruit.'"

**14.93.72**  "'For King Rantideva, having nothing, gave only water with a pure mind, O brāhmaṇa, and thereby went to the summit of heaven.'"

**14.93.73**  "'Righteousness is not so well pleased, my dear one, by gifts of great fruit that are given, as it is contented by slender gifts righteously obtained and hallowed by faith.'"

**14.93.74**  "'King Nṛga gave thousands of cows to brāhmaṇas, yet by giving away a single cow that belonged to another he came to hell.'"

**14.93.75**  "'By the gift of his own flesh, King Śibi, the son of Uśīnara, attained the worlds of those who have done meritorious deeds, and that man of firm vows rejoices in heaven.'"

**14.93.76**  In sheer abundance there is no merit for men; for the good, what is earned righteously by one's own power outweighs, O brāhmaṇa, the fruit gathered through manifold sacrifices.

**14.93.77**  Anger destroys the fruit of giving, and through greed one does not reach heaven; but the man of righteous conduct, knowing how to give, attains heaven through his austerity.

**14.93.78**  Not by many Rājasūya sacrifices offered with lavish gifts, nor by many Aśvamedhas, could a fruit be won equal to this of yours.

**14.93.79**  By a measure of barley-meal the world of Brahmā has been won by you, O sinless one; go, O brāhmaṇa, to the stainless abode of Brahmā as you wish.

**14.93.80**  For all of you, O best of the twice-born, a celestial chariot now stands ready; mount it at your pleasure. I am Dharma, O brāhmaṇa—behold me.

**14.93.81**  By you the body has been purified, and your fame stands firm in the world; with your wife, with your son, and with your daughter-in-law, go to heaven.

**14.93.82**  Thus addressed by Dharma, that brāhmaṇa mounted the chariot and, with his wife, his son, and his daughter-in-law, went up to heaven.

**14.93.83**  When that brāhmaṇa, knower of dharma, had gone to heaven with his son, his daughter-in-law, and his wife as the fourth, I then came forth from my burrow.

**14.93.84**  And then, by the scent of that barley-meal, by the moisture of the water, by the crushing of celestial flowers, by those very fragments of the good man's gift, and by that brāhmaṇa's austerity, my head was turned to gold.

**14.93.85**  And by the slender gift of that man true to his word, half my body too, O brāhmaṇas, was made of gold. Behold this vast transformation, wrought by the austerity of that wise one.

**14.93.86**  'How might my other side too become like this?'—so thinking, O twice-born, eagerly I visit again and again the groves of penance and the sites of sacrifice.

**14.93.87**  Hearing of this sacrifice of the wise king of the Kurus, I came with the highest hope—yet I have not been turned to gold.

**14.93.88**  Therefore, O best of the twice-born, laughing, I spoke these words: this sacrifice is by no means the equal of that measure of barley-meal.

**14.93.89**  For by those fragments of the barley-meal I was then turned to gold; this great sacrifice, in my judgment, is not their equal.

**14.93.90**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Having thus spoken to all the foremost brāhmaṇas at the sacrifice, the mongoose vanished from sight, O king, and those brāhmaṇas went to their homes.

**14.93.91**  All this I have related to you, O conqueror of hostile cities—the wonder that came to pass at that great horse-sacrifice.

**14.93.92**  You should feel no astonishment at all, O king, at the sacrifice; for thousands upon tens of millions of sages have gone to heaven through their austerities.

**14.93.93**  Harmlessness toward all beings, contentment, good conduct, uprightness, austerity, self-restraint, truth, and giving—these are held to be of equal worth.

**14.94.1**  Janamejaya said: Kings are devoted to sacrifice, the great sages devoted to austerity, and the brāhmaṇas set upon peace—'tranquillity and self-restraint,' so they say, O lord.

**14.94.2**  'Therefore nothing here is found to equal the fruits of sacrifice'—so my mind has held, and so without doubt it seemed to me.

**14.94.3**  For many kings, O best of the twice-born, having worshipped with sacrifices and won the highest fame here, departed hence to heaven after death.

**14.94.4**  The thousand-eyed king of the gods, mighty in splendour, the all-powerful one, attained by sacrifices with abundant gifts the entire sovereignty over the gods.

**14.94.5**  Just as King Yudhiṣṭhira, with Bhīma and Arjuna at his head, was the equal of the king of the gods in prosperity and in valour.

**14.94.6**  Then why did that mongoose disparage that rite, the Aśvamedha, the great sacrifice of that high-souled king?

**14.94.7**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: The foremost rule of sacrifice and its fruit, O bull among men, hear from me as I tell it truly here, O king, O Bhārata.

**14.94.8**  Long ago, while Śakra was sacrificing, all the great sages spoke out, when the priests were busy with their tasks and the rite of sacrifice was under way.

**14.94.9**  As the oblation was poured into the fire, the office of the hotṛ priest being richly endowed, the gods being summoned, and the supreme sages standing by.

**14.94.10**  By the brāhmaṇas then well assured, versed in their own scriptures and clear of voice, O king, and by the foremost adhvaryu priests, tireless and deft.

**14.94.11**  At that moment of immolation, when the sacrificial animals had been seized, the great sages, O mighty king, were filled with compassion.

**14.94.12**  Then, seeing the wretched animals, those sages rich in austerity came up to Śakra and said: 'This rule of sacrifice is not auspicious.'

**14.94.13**  'This is a misunderstanding on your part, who desire the great dharma; for herds of animals at a sacrifice are not what is sanctioned by the rule, O Purandara.'

**14.94.14**  'This undertaking of yours, O lord, is a destroyer of dharma; this is no dharma born of righteousness—violence is not called dharma.'

**14.94.15**  'Let them perform your sacrifice by scripture alone, if you wish it; by a sacrifice sanctioned by the rule your dharma would be very great.'

**14.94.16**  'Sacrifice with seeds, O thousand-eyed one, kept stored at most for three years; this is great dharma, O Śakra, attained when rightly pondered.'

**14.94.17**  But Śatakratu did not accept that counsel spoken by the sages who perceive the truth, for he was in thrall to pride and delusion.

**14.94.18**  A very great dispute arose between Śakra and the great sages—whether sacrifice should be made with moving creatures or with things that do not move, O Bhārata.

**14.94.19**  But those sages who perceive the truth, weary of the dispute, then, by agreement with Śakra, put the question to King Vasu.

**14.94.20**  'O greatly blessed king, what is the scriptural tradition handed down for sacrifices? Should one sacrifice with sacred animals, or rather with seeds and with that which is unborn?'

**14.94.21**  Hearing that question of theirs, without weighing its strength and weakness, the king declared: 'One should sacrifice with whatever is brought to hand.'

**14.94.22**  Having spoken thus, that king sank into the nether world; for he had uttered a falsehood here, O king—that lord and master of the Cedis.

**14.94.23**  Whoever, being unwise, though longing for dharma, sacrifices with wealth obtained by unrighteous means, gains no fruit of dharma.

**14.94.24**  And the man of evil soul who makes a snare of dharma, who gives gifts to brāhmaṇas merely to win the world's trust—

**14.94.25**  Such an unbridled twice-born, having gained wealth by sinful deed and possessed by passion and delusion, attains in the end a foul course.

**14.94.26**  The gifts bestowed by that sinful man of ruined judgment, however vast, find no firm ground and perish.

**14.94.27**  For that wicked-minded man given to unrighteousness, the cruel one of evil soul, there is no glory in his giving—neither here nor after death.

**14.94.28**  Indeed, the man bent on hoarding, in thrall to greed and delusion, of sinful mind, torments living beings with his violence.

**14.94.29**  He who, having gained wealth thus through greed, sacrifices and gives—by that deed he does not succeed, for it springs from ill-gotten means.

**14.94.30**  Gleaned grain, root, fruit, herb, or a vessel of water, O you rich in austerity—righteous men who give such a gift according to their means go to heaven.

**14.94.31**  This is great dharma: renunciation, giving, and compassion for living beings; chastity, truth, mercy, steadfastness, and forbearance—this is the eternal root of the eternal dharma.

**14.94.32**  For we hear, O brāhmaṇas, of kings of old, Viśvāmitra and the rest—Viśvāmitra and Asita, Janaka the lord of earth, Kakṣasena and Ārṣṭiṣeṇa, and Sindhudvīpa the king.

**14.94.33**  These and many other kings, rich in austerity, attained the highest perfection through truth and through gifts gained by righteous means.

**14.94.34**  Brāhmaṇas, kṣatriyas, vaiśyas, and śūdras who have taken to austerity—purified by the fire of giving and dharma, they go to heaven, O Bhārata.

**14.95.1**  Janamejaya said: If, O reverend one, all is attainable through renunciation that comes by righteous means, then declare all this to me, for you are skilled in speaking.

**14.95.2**  The great fruit that came of the gift of barley-meal by one living on gleaned grain has been told me as great, O brāhmaṇa; this is true beyond doubt.

**14.95.3**  How then, among all sacrifices, would the supreme certainty be reached? This you ought to tell me in full, O bull among the twice-born.

**14.95.4**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: On this they recount too this ancient tale, of what came to pass long ago at the great sacrifice of Agastya, O subduer of foes.

**14.95.5**  Long ago, Agastya of great splendour, devoted to the welfare of all beings, entered upon a consecration of twelve years, O mighty king.

**14.95.6**  At the rite of that high-souled one the officiating priests were like fire itself—living on roots, or taking no food at all, grinding grain with stones, or drinking only the rays of the sun.

**14.95.7**  Some ate only what was ground fine, some only the leavings of others, some kept naught for the morrow; and ascetics and mendicants too were there, stationed all about.

**14.95.8**  All of them had dharma made manifest in them, had conquered anger and subdued their senses; all were established in self-restraint, free of hypocrisy and delusion.

**14.95.9**  Ever fixed in pure conduct, untroubled by the senses, those great sages attended upon that sacrifice and partook of its food.

**14.95.10**  That food the reverend one had procured according to his power; and at that rite nothing whatever was unfit, for great sacrifices have been performed thus by many sages.

**14.95.11**  But while the great sacrifice of Agastya, of such a kind, was going on, the thousand-eyed one did not send rain, O best of the Bharatas.

**14.95.12**  Then, in an interval of the rite of the high-souled Agastya, O king, this discourse arose among the sages of disciplined souls.

**14.95.13**  'Agastya, the sacrificer, gives food without grudging; yet the rain-cloud sends no rain—how will there be food?'

**14.95.14**  'And this great rite of the sage, O brāhmaṇas, lasts twelve years; and the god will not send rain for these twelve years.'

**14.95.15**  'Considering this, you ought to do a kindness for this wise great sage, Agastya of surpassing austerity.'

**14.95.16**  When these words had been thus spoken, Agastya the mighty, eloquent of speech, won the sages over with a bow of his head and said this:

**14.95.17**  'If for twelve years Vāsava sends no rain, I shall perform the sacrifice of thought; this is the eternal rule.'

**14.95.18**  'If for twelve years Vāsava sends no rain, by my own exertion I shall accomplish other sacrifices of surpassing rigour.'

**14.95.19**  'This sacrifice of mine with seeds, gathered over many years—I shall carry it out with prepared seeds, and here there will be no hindrance.'

**14.95.20**  'This rite of mine cannot be made vain by any means, whether the god sends rain here or whether he does not.'

**14.95.21**  'Or else Indra may grant my entreaty—but not by his mere pleasure; I myself shall become Indra and give life to the creatures.'

**14.95.22**  'Whatever each being was born to feed upon, that it shall have just so; and beyond this I shall make abundance, again and again, exceedingly.'

**14.95.23**  'This day let gold come hither, and whatever other wealth is hard to win; whatever exists in the three worlds, let it come here of its own accord.'

**14.95.24**  'And the celestial throngs of apsarases, with the gandharvas and the kiṃnaras, Viśvāvasu and the others too—let them ever wait upon you.'

**14.95.25**  'And whatever wealth there is among the Northern Kurus, let it all present itself here of its own accord at my sacrifice—heaven, and the dwellers in heaven, and Dharma himself as well.'

**14.95.26**  When he had spoken thus, all this came to pass for that wise one. Then those sages, beholding the power of that sage's austerity, were amazed and all spoke this word of deep meaning:

**14.95.27**  'We are pleased by your word, yet we do not wish for this expenditure of austerity; we are content with our own sacrifices, and we desire only what comes by righteous means.'

**14.95.28**  'Sacrifices, consecrations, oblations, and whatever else we seek—let that be ours through sacrifices of our own making; we do not seek it from elsewhere.'

**14.95.29**  We win our food by lawful means, devoted to our own duties, and we seek to master the Vedas through chastity, in accordance with right law.

**14.95.30**  And in due course, having lawfully gone forth from our homes, we shall practice austerity by the prescribed gateways that are sanctioned by righteousness.

**14.95.31**  This resolve of yours is wholly right, free from all violence; proclaim this non-injury at sacrifices forever, O lord.

**14.95.32**  Then we shall be well pleased, O best of the twice-born; and being released at the conclusion, we shall depart from this sacrificial session.

**14.95.33**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Even as they were speaking thus, the king of the gods, Puraṃdara of mighty splendor, beholding the power of his austerity, sent down rain.

**14.95.34**  And before the sacrifice was concluded, O Janamejaya, Indra, lord of the gods, of measureless valor, became a rainer of showers at will.

**14.95.35**  And the lord of the thirty gods himself came forth, O royal sage, and, placing Bṛhaspati before him, conciliated that Agastya.

**14.95.36**  Then, at the close of the sacrifice, Agastya, supremely pleased, honored those great sages according to rule and dismissed them.

**14.96.1**  Janamejaya said: Who was that, in the form of a mongoose with a golden head, who spoke in a human voice? Tell me this, for I ask you.

**14.96.2**  Vaiśaṃpāyana said: Of this I was not asked before, nor was it told by us. Let it be heard who that mongoose was, and how his speech was human.

**14.96.3**  In olden days, it is said, Jamadagni resolved to perform a śrāddha; the cow of oblations came to him, and he himself milked her.

**14.96.4**  He set that milk in a new vessel, firm and clean; and Anger, in his own embodied shape, overturned the pot.

**14.96.5**  Wishing to test that best of sages—what would he do when an offense was given—that evil-minded one, so reflecting, defiled the milk.

**14.96.6**  But the sage, recognizing him as Anger, was not in the least angered at him; whereupon that Anger, taking embodied form and standing with joined palms, said this to him:

**14.96.7**  "I am conquered, O best of the Bhṛgus! It is a false report in the world that the Bhṛgus are exceedingly prone to wrath, for I have been vanquished by you."

**14.96.8**  "Now today I stand at your mercy, who are forbearing and great of soul; I dread your austerity, O virtuous one. Show me your grace, O mighty lord."

**14.96.9**  Jamadagni said: "You have been seen by me face to face, O Anger. Go, with your fever gone. You have done me no wrong this day, and there is no resentment in me."

**14.96.10**  "But those for whose sake this resolve concerning the milk was made by me—they are your forefathers, the greatly blessed; turn to them for understanding, and let you go."

**14.96.11**  Thus addressed, struck with terror, he vanished there; and through the curse-like contact of the forefathers he attained the state of a mongoose.

**14.96.12**  He propitiated them, that there might be an end to the curse; and he was told by them: "When you shall revile Dharma, then you shall be released."

**14.96.13**  So bidden by them, scorning the regions sacred to sacrifice and the forests of righteousness, ranging about, he came at last to that sacrifice.

**14.96.14**  Then, having reviled the son of Dharma over that measure of barley-meal, he, that Anger, was freed from the curse; for Dharma himself was Yudhiṣṭhira.

**14.96.15**  Thus did this come to pass at the sacrifice of that great-souled one; and there, even as we looked on, the mongoose vanished away.
